years and it is the reason a register kept from that sheet has five columns and the third of them has nothing over it and what is in it is a hand and not a name.** Then: **then it is a form anybody could use.** No: **it is a form nobody has ever been entitled to use, and a second person cannot be served, and being served is what makes a person a party, and a party is the only person who can be told what a thing is going to be used for, so the fifth line asks a question of the one person in the room who by definition cannot be told, and it has been dead for about a hundred and forty years and nobody took it out and nobody ever found out it was dead.** And it was done once: in pencil, in a kitchen, in about nine minutes, in the first month of the year after, by her, who is a party and therefore the only person entitled to ask, who said the price out loud before she asked, and who asked a woman of sixty-three in her own kitchen whether she was a party to a thing about to be said in a room, and got *no*, and wrote the no in pencil on that woman's table, and a girl of nineteen wrote it out fair, and there is a sheet of paper in that kitchen with that woman's name on it and it is not the office's name. **It will not be done again on that woman: there is no form in this empire for asking a person the same question twice, and if there were one the second asking would be about the first, and she would be asked in a room whether she remembers, and a person remembering a thing and a person reading a thing back are two different acts and only one of them is evidence.** Two refusals: what the certification had cost her, given in the same instrument a woman of twenty used about a different figure four hundred and thirty miles away in a different month; and the name of the woman in the third column — **a person who is in a book in a house about four miles off a road and whose name is not in it, and if I say the name in this room then a person in a book becomes a name in a room, and the man who printed the nine lines of that woman's own hand at fourpence a copy did not print her, and I am not going to be the person who says it out loud.** The clerk of about twenty-five who took the minute wrote down without being asked that no form in this matter had ever been put to a person and been asked about, and that the reason a form printed in about nine hundred buildings had done what it did for about a hundred and forty years was that nobody had ever stood in front of a heading and asked what a word on it meant, and read it back to her before writing it, which is not the practice in that room, and asked to have *you are not the practice in this room* entered as well. Nobody thanked her. She took the sheet away because exhibits go back to the person who brought them, and it went into a book with no office on it on a page with the day on it, **and a book with no office on it is a thing a form cannot be answered with, and that is the only kind of record she has ever had, and it has cost her nine seconds and it is worth nothing and she is going to keep it.**

## Chapter 193 — remainder of the entry

She is served on the second day of the third week, at the window about four feet off the ground, by a man who carries notices and who said the word *served* out loud, which he is required to do and which he said properly. **All four of the things are about a man called Pell Vey, and she is the only person in that building who has ever had to write any of them down, and she wrote them all down.** **Being served has stopped being a cost. Being documented is a different thing and it is not cheaper**, and she priced that out loud in the corridor about four minutes after he had gone down it, to nobody. One: a delivery stub, a slip the size of a playing card with a day on it, two numbers, and a line at the foot with a hand on it, **and a delivery stub is the one document in this empire designed to be handed to a person who is not the person it is about.** She put the hand beside four others within the hour and it took about nine minutes: **it is Pell Vey, head of the Notaries' Table, holder of the cabinet key, and there is not one document in that building with his hand on it that she has ever been able to read, and there is one now, and a man who puts his own hand at the foot of a thing that is going to be carried to a building four hundred yards from his own is a man who has decided to be findable, and in nine years she has not once seen an office do it.** **A hand identified by comparison is a hand nobody can deny, and that is the first time in this matter that anything has been a document against a person who is alive and in a building.** Two: a file kept in four places, on paper, in his own hand, and the list came to her on the fourth day with the same man and the same word at the sill. Three of the four lines are buildings she can name. **The fourth place is a back room off a corridor she has walked every working day for nine years, and she has never once been in it, and there is no list anywhere in that building that says who is allowed in it.** The man who carries notices says he does not know what is in it and is not going to tell her. Three: on three days, on a form with a question on it, the same person was asked the same question and refused, **and the words were fourteen words long and they were his own and they did not vary by a word.** **A refusal in one form on three days is a person being consistent, and a person being consistent is a document**, and the other half took her nine minutes: **a man in that building could have refused in four different forms and been unfindable, and he did not, and there is no form anywhere that requires a person to be consistent, and the fact that he was is the most exposed thing about him that anybody has found this year, and nobody made him do it and he cannot now unsay it.** Nobody asked her for that; she said so twice and wrote it down. Four: she went to the back room on the fifth day, because a file in four places can be produced and a file that can be produced will be asked about. There was nothing in it. **In nine years she has been able to say she did not know what the presses were for; she can now say she knows what the fourth place is for, and that it is for a person with a pen to sit at a table with, and a person with a pen at a table can put a thing into a column and take a thing out of a column, and both of those are the same hand and the same table, and there is no instrument anywhere that stops it.** What had been done there was in a margin, in a copy of a roll, in a hand that was not his, with a day against it — the fourth day of the fourth week of the ninth month of next year — correcting a line in which a body stood in a column headed for the person who did a work and putting a person's name there instead, and writing underneath why, and signing it. **It is nine minutes of work, it is the only thing in the whole of this matter that is a record on purpose and not a record by accident, and it was given away by a provincial archivist on the ordinary round with a box on her hip and a list of eleven stops, who came for four days and was not paid for it because nobody asked and there is no form for it, and who was not thanked and has not been thanked and nobody is going to thank her, and who said in that back room in words that it is not a crime and does not make her a good person.** **She does not know that her hand is in a fourth place of a file in a building about four hundred and thirty miles from her own office, and she does not know that a clerk of thirty-eight has spent about nine minutes in a corridor matching her writing to three other hands, and she does not know that a man is on a form three times in three days refusing in the same fourteen words because of a line in a margin.** The same surname is not a door and has never been one, and telling the man who carries notices would be a service and being served is a party. Then a scene at the sill: a man of about thirty-four with a fortnight's wage held, and **he is not on the form, and if he were he would be a person in a box with a heading over it, and a person in a box can be asked about for the rest of their life, and there is no form anywhere for taking a person back out of a box** — so the thing holding his wage has protected him from being findable and cost him fourteen days of a man's work, and both are true. He wants it written down twice; **she cannot, has looked for a form for it that morning and there is not one, and this is the first time she has wanted a form and not found one and it is the worst morning she has had there since she came**; she puts it in her own book with the day on it and her own hand, and that is a record of her saying it to him and not a record of him, and it is worth exactly what it is worth. He asked for the second reading and she gave it. Nobody thanked her. She wrote it all down that evening in her own book, read it back twice, the second slower, and did not read it a third time, and wrote at the bottom the reason: **a hand of hers is the only hand anybody is going to be able to put side by side in about four years, and she has now done this three times and the reason has not got any better and she has not stopped.** **The finding of it is not a man and it is not a woman and it is a hand: two of the four places have a name on the foot of them and one of them does not, and the one that does not is the one that is going to cost somebody something, and the somebody is not in this matter and does not know they are in it.**

## Chapter 194 — remainder of the entry

The counting room is about eleven feet by fourteen with a bench, a window with a broken light, a stove she does not light until the ninth month, and about forty books on shelves made for taller books. **A district checks its roll of households because a district has to know who is in it, and the check is in the ordinary way and the ordinary way is what breaks it.** A woman of about sixty who has kept that roll for nineteen years comes down the lane on the second day of the fourth week with a sheet on a board and a basket, asks the fourth and the seventh in order, is told the room has not changed and is one person, and then turns the board round. **The something else said *representative in a matter*, against her name, with a day against it in a hand she did not know, and the day was the fourth day of the third week of the fifth month, entered in the office of a seat, and no further consent of the person required.** She reads it standing up and the woman says nothing, because a woman who keeps a roll for nineteen years knows what it is to stand in a lane and say nothing while somebody reads. Then: **I am not blaming you and you did not do it and you are the reason I can stand here and say what I am about to say out loud**; and if she had not come on the second day of the fourth week she would not have found out for a season, **and a season is about the length a thing can be held for without anybody deciding it is a season.** Then she says no, in a room, out loud, with the day on it, **which is the only place in this empire a no has ever worked**, and the woman of about sixty says she can put it in the fourth column of the roll and it will be there for nineteen years. **Write the day. Write that I said it out loud in this room with you in it. Write that I did not consent. Do not write why, and do not write a reason, because there is no box for a why on a roll of households and I have looked at the roll in your hand and there is nothing on this page for one.** And it did not work, and she had known it would not before she said it: **no further consent required. A room that writes a no down is not the thing that has the no in it.** She is refusing to be served, **the only instrument there is, used once already, not a lawyer, not proud of it, using it now**, and she asks that to be written down too. **The entry was made by a clerk about nine miles from a counter, filling a box the way the box is printed; she was not a villain and she was not asked and she did it correctly, and I cannot get at her and there is no form in this empire that lets a person produce a thing that is not in a book with a name on the spine.** The hearing is about four hundred and thirty miles up this river, on the third day of the third week of this month, **and I am going to it, and I am not paid to go and nobody is paying for me to go and I am not going to add it up.** The woman of about sixty at the eleventh door then says that the word *hold* has been used twice and that she has been left out of it twice, **and I told you in the second week of the third month that I would rather be asked than not, and you told me you would not be able to do that for me and that it was much smaller, and it was, and here it is again, and I have not been asked either** — and goes home, and is not thanked, and there is no form that requires it. Then the count. **The entry was made on the fourth day of the third week of the fifth month. That is a day.** A form that cannot be asked can still be filled in, and the day it was filled in is a day, and **the day is the only part of the whole thing that anybody can find.** She writes the day on the second clean sheet under the heading she wrote herself, and then stops and looks at it, and leaves it. **That sheet says what a person is owed, and by whom, and on what day, about the eleven houses and the four other houses and about nobody else**, and she started it because a column with a heading over it is a machine for turning one thing into another kind of thing and she wanted to see whether she could aim one on purpose; it is hers, nobody asked for it, no form, no fee, no column in that town will take it. **A day is a fact. A day on a sheet of her own with her own heading on it is a record of a person, and a record of a person is the easiest thing in this empire to make, and she has spent three years refusing to make one.** She does not tear it out; she leaves the day on the sheet, the first entry on it and the only one about anybody in her family. Then the weekly count, done out of her head in about four minutes, and **it has one line per house and has had for three years, and there is a second thing it could have now, which is a line for what that house is owed, and she has the figure for nine houses and not for the eleventh, because the eleventh is hers and she has never written down what she is owed.** She could have made it two columns in about a minute. **A column is a machine and a second column is two machines, and a person who has one machine and can see the whole of it knows what it is for, and a person with two of them has to choose which one she is looking at, and choosing is a thing she does not do in a book.** She leaves it one column, does not write the eleventh house at all, and says out loud to an empty counting room: **a book is a room, and I am not going to start filling one in on myself.** On the sixth day she writes a fifth thing in the fourth book of the dyers, in the fourth column, with no name in it, in about four lines, and says in the book that the reason the name is not in it is four words and they are the same four words as last time, and that she said them out loud to her brother in a street about four hundred and thirty miles away and has never seen him write them down, and **I want it written down as well, because a person reads a book after four years and not before, and in four years this page will say that a woman said no and will not say what the no did.** She reads it back once and not twice, because there is nobody in the room. And **a district clerk of about sixty walked down a lane with a board and asked her about a place of custom or of work, in the ordinary way, and got the ordinary answer, and wrote it down, and will do it again at the same time next year. There is nothing in that which anybody can be angry with and there is everything in it.**

## Chapter 195 — remainder of the entry

She is not a villain and has never sent a person to a jail. **She has a marble thing about a rule and about keeping people in roofs, and she gave it up on the third day of the fourth week of the fifth month, in about nine minutes, in front of a room, and what she gave up was not the rule.** They start with the four files. *Why four.* **Because I was asked what I could bring and I counted, and I chose them because I know which of them will hurt me and I have brought those.** Then the river: it was in the first of two, both in the same flood season, both chosen by hand, both with a day on them, **and she can name the year and cannot name the file.** **There were about a hundred and thirty people in front of me who could have been held. There was a river coming. I held about forty of them.** Chose forty out of a hundred and thirty. And why: **because a predictable claim in the right place stops an eviction in the wrong place** — said without any weight, read out exactly as written because *in about four years somebody is going to come in here and say a good reason was applied unevenly and I would rather the sentence were in the room than my memory of it* — **and the reasoning is the reasoning every flood authority in every district in this empire uses and has always used. It is not a bad rule. It is a good rule. That is not the thing that is wrong with it.** Then the witness, who had not come to be one: **Wren Ludd, about seventy-four**, who came because a paper with a day on it and a carriage paid for arrived, and **the carriage is not a wage and the paying of it is not a kindness and both of those facts are on the record.** He gives his name and the rest of it and watches her write it. *You are one of the ninety.* **I am one of the ninety. I had somewhere to go. That is the whole of the reason and it took her a flood season to work out which ninety and it took me nineteen years to be able to say this in a room.** The place was four miles up a lane on the low side and it belonged to a man whose ground had water standing in it from the second day of that week; he was on the high side of the same lane with a mother in the house and about nine feet of floor between the front door and the back. **He died in the fourth week. I have not said that in a room in nineteen years and I have said it to about four people on that lane and I am saying it here because she is in front of me and I am not going to make her say it first.** He did not know the man; he asks the reason be written as the reason he is sitting there and not as a reason about her, and she asks for it in those words. Then the finding. **In nineteen years I have enforced continuation clauses against about nine hundred and forty people in this district — not in one flood, in about a hundred and forty matters — and in the same nineteen years I have not enforced them against about four hundred.** The first category: about two hundred and ten who could not be found afterwards, and the service says they were served, and no court in this empire has ever gone back. The second: about a hundred and forty who are of a household, **and I entered *household* in the third line of about a hundred and forty files because there was a column for it and nothing else in the line would take.** The third, never written down until now: **about two hundred and forty people in front of me in the second of the two floods, and I held about nine of them, and I chose those nine because they were the nine who had water standing in their ground and everybody else had somewhere to go.** I chose, both times, by hand, on a day, in my district. Then: **a good reason applied unevenly by one person in a district for nineteen years is not a rule and cannot be defended as one. A rule is a thing that is applied the same way each time. This was not applied the same way each time. I chose, and the reason I chose is good, and the second four hundred are the reason the first nine hundred and forty cannot be defended as a rule.** She asks for it in those words, and for **I am not naming anybody**, and gives the reason again, the same reason as before and not a better one: **a person who names a body in a minute becomes a person whose minute can be asked about, and a minute that can be asked about is a document.** And then the thing she volunteers: **a finding entered against a person by that person on that person's own motion names no person, is appealable by nobody, and is not a discovery and is not a seam. It cannot be discovered, it cannot be unseated, it cannot be carried somewhere else and used. It binds me and it helps nobody, and I have made it mine so that it is here.** The court records it. Then the witness is asked what he wants, thinks about it long enough that the clerk stops writing, and says he is not owed anything; **that is the first time anybody has told him why he did not drown, and it cost her nothing to say it and it cost me the carriage and I would not have come without the carriage and I am not going to pretend otherwise.** He says four sentences and nobody writes them down because nobody asked him to, and goes. **The shift is in the record and it is not absolution and it is not a conversion, and the difference is that nothing in this room has made anybody safer, and she has not been forgiven by anybody and has not asked and there is no form that requires it to be entered that she has not been forgiven.** Nobody thanked her and she said in advance that nobody was to. And about four hundred and thirty miles down this river a foreman of fifty-one has never once in nine years been asked to read anything by anybody at the district works, and a seat's paper arrived with her name at the top of it in the third week of the fourth month, and she has not opened it, and there is no form anywhere that says she has to.

## Chapter 196 — remainder of the entry

**The mark on the inside of his left wrist has not done anything since the fourth hour of the second of the fifth month of next year, when it was up for about nine seconds, and it told nobody anything, and it is not the price, and the wage is the price.** What he is doing is the thing nobody in this matter has been able to do: **you do not reconstruct a person by remembering her, you reconstruct a person's reasoning by walking it, step by step, in the order she would have walked it, out of the terms she had, and if the terms and the order both hold then the person is inside the reconstruction and not behind it.** **It can only be done by somebody who can hold four terms at once, and there is no form in this empire that says a person may do it**, and his only instrument is the two-inch line on his wrist that reads nothing. The reconstruction is the order of a day: a person in a room, a second person in the room, the question, the answer in a box, the day in the first person's own hand, the thing said, the first person not reading it back, the second person writing what was said and nothing else and not reading it back and not showing it. **He walked it eleven times and the results were the same eleven times.** Then: **A procedure is not a person. A record of what a person did in a room is a record of the shape of the room and the order the things were asked in. The order is knowable. The shape is knowable. What a person was in that room to do is in neither of them, and the reason she was in the room is the only part anybody has ever wanted, and it is the part a procedure cannot carry.** It is the part a form printed in nine hundred buildings was built to stop, **and the building of it was not done by a villain and was done in about nine minutes by a person filling in a box.** And the second half: **the procedure is not a machine for hiding the reason. The procedure is a machine for making sure the reason is not needed, and it does that by not asking, and the not-asking is in the first line.** Then he reaches for the other instrument and it does nothing: **it reads nothing, it decides nothing, it does not know where the ford is, it has never known anything, and there is no office in this empire whose business it is to ask it anything. It is a symptom.** The price is about forty-four pounds a year and five journeys and thirty days and three pounds and thirteen shillings and threepence gone out of it with no fund and no form for a fund. **He was not disappointed, and he wrote that down, because a man who is not disappointed is a man who expected something.** And then the day went, in the middle of the ninth of the eleven walks, and nobody took it and nobody noticed it go. **The sixth day of the third month. He was eleven. His mother put a sheet of paper into a box on a kitchen table and told him to leave the room, and he has written it down twice and told it to four people, and it is the first thing in a book with no office on it that has four pages headed *What the blankness is for* and a second heading *Written by a person it was done to, who was not asked*, and it is the whole of what he knows about where the idea came from.** **He can say the date and he can say he was eleven and he can say there was a kitchen and a table and a box, and he cannot say whether he left the room or stood in the doorway. He told that story in a rented room in the fourth week of the fourth month, out loud, to a woman of twenty-four, and it is gone, and it is not coming back, and there is no form anywhere in this empire for a person to have a day back.** He noticed it at about the ninth hour by going back to the fourth page and finding a line about the district works in his own handwriting and no memory of writing it. **Nobody comforted him.** She was in the room for the whole of it and there are two reasons and **they are two instruments and they have never been the same instrument**: a paper, made in a form, in four days, with a day on it, requiring her in any room he is in, producible by anybody holding it; and a watch, a separate instrument, requiring only that a person who is not from the office that cannot be named be in any room he is in. **Both of them require her.** A person who is safe because nobody can instruct her and useful because somebody needs her standing in a room is a person with two boxes filled in on the same form, **and she wrote that sentence down months ago and has not said it out loud to him until this month and she is not going to say it tonight.** She did not stop working: a bundle of about four hundred forms on the corner of the table going into a second order because somebody asked for a second order, and she went on going through them. He picked up a sheet — the sheet a witness is entered upon, the same five lines read out in a hearing room in the second week of the fifth month by a clerk of thirty-four who is a party — **and he is not going to read it out again because he has it by heart and there is nobody in this room who needs it**, and she tells him, without looking up, that he has checked that sheet eleven times since the second month in a corridor and in a rented room and once in a bay four hundred and thirty miles down a river with a lamp behind her, **and that about four hundred forms compared fifth line against fifth line with not one difference is the most thorough piece of work anybody has done on that document in about a hundred and forty years, and it is work a person with a good eye and a lamp can do and no instrument whatever, and he has never once been thanked for it and he is not going to be** — and then: **you did it for your mother and you have been doing it four hundred times since and the two are not the same, and a fact said in a room with two people in it at the eleventh hour is not the only kind of fact this empire has.** She gives him a fact when he asks: the price of a party out loud, **the ninth time, eight of the nine about a person, this one about about four hundred forms, and she has not said it since the fourth month and has not missed it**. And then the fact that is about him: **a day of your own memory is not evidence. It is not in a copy and it is not in a hand anybody can compare and there is no form in this empire for producing it, and the one thing you had that no document in this matter has is the one thing that can be taken out of a book with a pen and a good reason. And there is a book in this matter with about four hundred lines in it about four miles off a road, and it has more of your mother in it than you are going to have again, and you cannot go and get it this week and you have not been able to since the second month.** **Nobody comforted him, and Tamsin Rook did not put her own work down** — she finished the second order at about the eleventh hour, wrote the day against the top sheet in her own hand, and did not show it to him and did not read it out. Then the finding, written out fair and read back twice, second slower, nobody in the room to hear either: **the reconstruction is possible, out of a procedure and out of nothing else, and it will give a shape and not a woman, and that is not a defect in the method, that is the method. I am going to do it anyway. A man who does a thing that cannot give him what he wants is doing a thing that is not heroism and is not cowardice and has no name on any form in this empire, and I have looked, and there is no form for it, and I am going to do it on the fourth day of next week.** Nobody thanked him.

## Chapter 197 — remainder of the entry

**She entered a date four years off in the first week of the fifth month and did not know what she was entering, and she has never once been asked what any of it meant.** She was asked on the second day of the third week, in four words, by a person with no office in this matter; she said yes; she was asked again on the third day and said yes again; and on the fourth day a person asked her whether she understood what she was writing down, and she said she was going to find out and report. The two sentences, in this order, with nothing between them: **the witness status of a person is restored, the record having been wrongly kept, and the restoration is entered with a day on it and a name at the foot of it, and the name goes on a roll, and there is no way in this empire to put a name back a second time.** **An act done by a person who was not heard cannot be undone by a court that has no power over her and no standing to make one.** The first is a thing a court can do because the court found the record wrong. The second is a thing no court in this empire can do, and the reason is not unwillingness and not lack of power, it is that **the person is not alive to be heard, and there is no form anywhere in this empire for a person to be heard after, and the not-hearing is the finding and it cannot be got round by anybody in this room by any instrument anybody has.** And before she reads them: **I am going to read two sentences in this order and I am not going to stop between them, and if anybody in this room thinks the first one makes the second one better then they should leave now, because they will be here for the rest of it and there is nothing in the rest of it either.** Nobody left. Then the act, described without a word for it: **a box on a form printed in nine hundred buildings, filled in by a person with authority to fill boxes, in a room, with a second person in it who was not a party and could not ask anything; nine lines in a woman's hand, on the page whole in a public document at fourpence a copy with a heading that says what it is and where it came from and a man's name at the foot of it and no date and four lines arguing why there is no date; and the last of the nine says it is to be done on a box and not on a paper, because a paper goes to a house and a box goes to a roll.** **The box went to a roll. That is what happened to it and it is the only thing that has happened to it, and there is no instrument in this empire that can be asked what it was for by anybody who was in the room, because the only person who was in the room and was not the person being written about is in nine hundred books under no heading at all, and nobody is going to ask her again.** Then the roll itself: **a bound book about four inches thick with a marbled board and a clasp, in a press in that building for nineteen years; it is a roll of persons; a name goes onto it when a record about that person has been found to be wrongly kept; about nine hundred names have gone onto it in about a hundred and forty years; this is the first one in nineteen years.** She asks for a different pen because the first had been in a pocket, and writes the name in her own hand in the ordinary place with the day against it, and does not hurry and does not go back over it. **Nobody in that room had seen a name go onto that roll in about nineteen years, and a name only goes onto it when a court has found that a record about that person was wrongly kept, and there is no other door into that book, and a person who was never entered as a person in any record has nothing to be restored to.** She reads it back once and not twice, and somebody at the back says *the rule is twice*, and she says, without turning round, that it is a rule of a bay about four hundred and thirty miles down this river that a girl of seventeen made in the second week of the fourth month and nobody decided, and she is not going to decide it in a room with a rail in it, **and the day is in it twice anyway, once in my hand and once in the margin.** Tamsin Rook asks whether it is findable: **it is on a roll in a press in a building in a city and anybody with a copy of the page and about four years can put a hand of mine side by side with about four hundred others and find it. Findable is not a consolation. Findable is what a name on a roll is, and there is no other way to put one back, and a court that has found that a record was wrongly kept has got one instrument and it is a pen, and I have used it, and I would like the room to understand that I have used it and that nobody in this room is going to be thanked for it and I am not going to be either.** Then the refusal, entered with a reason so the not-entering is on the record and is not a silence: **there is a question in front of me about whether a seat of this empire gave an order in this matter. I cannot answer it. Nobody in this room can answer it, and it is not going to be answered in about four years by anybody, and the reason is not modesty and it is not caution. It is that the only document that would bear on it is a document that cannot be produced by anybody.** Asked what document: **a notice of a seat in six numbered lines with a device cut into the head of it and a seal at the foot of it, and under the seal there was a name once.** It is in that building, and she is not going to say the name, describe the hand, or say which of the two notices it is, and she asks for it to be entered that she declined and was asked and was not pressed. And the reason for the whole of it, on the table since the second week of the second month and never read aloud: **a document arrived in that building about four hours before a second one did, and the second one had a seal and nothing under the seal, and it asked in its own second line that no inference be drawn from the absence of any name. The arrival of the second document is the proof.** That is the whole of what the name at the foot of the first one was for, and it took one year and a printer and a piece of paper at fourpence a copy, and nobody did anything wrong in either of them. Nobody thanked her. Then her own sentence, nineteen years long, never said out loud: **a seat is a thing in a room without being a person. I have written nineteen years of minutes about a seat and I could not tell you one single thing about what a seat is, and I have never been in the same room as one, and there is no post in this empire whose business it is to be in the same room as one. I am the only person in this room who cannot be examined, and I am the only person in this room who is paid nothing, and those are the same reason and nobody has ever put them together.** And: **the finding is about a record and a roll and a name. The seat is not in the finding. Naming a thing in a room does not put the thing in the room, and there is no form in this empire that makes a document a person, and a document that names an office is a document and not an office.** Then a question is named. A question had been put to a court in this city about nine years ago, in a room with a number on the door, and the court still exists and sits on the same ground. **"The Cinder Clause," said Orla Denning**, once, correctly, and stopped, and the court recorded that the question exists. **The question exists. It was asked nine years ago by the court that asked it. It has not been answered in nine years by the court that asked it**, and nobody in the room has been asked it and nobody is going to answer it, and she says not one word about what it means because she does not know and there is nothing in the eleven books in that building that says. Entered in those words, and: **a document is findable, and I am going to put it in a minute book with the day on it and my name at the foot of it, and that is the only protection a question has ever had in this empire.** Then Tamsin Rook, who is in the room and is not a witness and cannot be examined and is not on any roll of anybody in this matter, and both instruments require her: the price of a party, **the tenth time**, nine of the ten about a person and this one about a name on a roll, **which is the only thing she has ever been in a room about that she cannot get out of by leaving the room**, with the number, four years and a hand of hers and about eleven weeks of standing in a corridor. Told she is not on the roll: **the woman who is not on the roll is sixty-three and she is in this city, and she cannot be put on that roll by this court or by any other, because the roll is a roll of persons and she was never entered as one.** The court rose at about the ninth hour. **A name was restored to a roll with a day on it and a name at the foot of it, and it is findable, and there is no way in this empire to put a name back a second time. An act done by a person who was not heard was not undone, and cannot be, and the not-undoing is entered with a day on it and a name at the foot of it, and it is findable too. And there is no way in this empire to un-undo one either, and that is the half of it that nobody is going to write about, and a reader is going to be left with two facts and no way of telling which of them this is about, and that is not an accident of the finding and it is not a defect in the record. It is what a room is.** Nobody thanked anybody, and about four hundred and thirty miles down this river a second season is in its fourth month and has not been asked of anybody.

## Chapter 198 — remainder of the entry

**She has put her own name at the foot of three lists of works and it is the first name at the foot of anything on that cut in sixty years.** **She can read. That is not in dispute and has never been and is in nine years of a shed nobody's business, and it is the other thing about her that is not in dispute: nobody at the district works has ever once in nine years asked her to read anything.** The paper came up the road in the third week of the fourth month on a cart with a boy and not with anybody who knew what was in it, with a seat's device cut into the head of it, no office on the wrapper, **her name at the top of it in the ordinary way**, and it sat in a box on a shelf in that shed for about eleven weeks. The shed is about nine foot by eleven, with a door that shuts, a bench along the north wall that four men can sit on, and a lamp not lit until about the seventh hour. **About nine men came in on the second day of the first week of the seventh month because somebody put a word in the yard and not because anybody asked them, and two of them came late on purpose.** She knew why, said so afterwards in the yard to the man who had come down the lane and to nobody in the shed: **they said no in the first season, both of them, at the gate, in the same words anybody uses, with a reason, and the reason went in the book in the place where the no is, and they have not been asked since and they are not going to be asked. They came late because they wanted to hear what I said before they decided whether to still mean it. That is not a thing a person is supposed to be able to do and they have done it and I am not going to make them explain.** And: **if they say it again it is because somebody asked them again, and nobody is going to ask them again until the season comes round, and when the season comes round somebody is going to have to. And that somebody is going to be me, because I hold the list, and the day somebody hands me a season to ask about is the day I am asking a person who has already said no once.** And: **forty-two years on this bank and nine years holding the only job on it that is written down nowhere, and there is no form in this empire that makes a person be in charge of a thing, and the nine families agreed it out loud about eleven years ago in a shed and it cannot be produced, and there is nobody in this district who would know how to write it down.** He had not got a book; she said to tell it to the man of thirty-eight, who will write it down for nothing, and she is not going to thank him; and: **everybody on this bank has got his name. That is the whole of what a name at the foot of a list is.** Then: **nobody sent her, nobody asked her, there is no form in this empire for a person who comes on her own account, and the whole of what she wanted was to be asked something in a room where she had to stand there while she said it.** She says before anybody has said anything that she has been asked about the bank five times this year and said no five times and every one was right, and has said in that shed that she would rather be asked than not, and has been asked twice and said yes twice, and has refused to be asked a third time without being asked. The man who came down the lane says he cannot arrange it and is not going to pretend he can: **the reason I am in this shed is that a thing happened in a city four hundred and thirty miles up this river and I have read about it, and I cannot get there, and I am not going to send anybody.** So they ask her. It took about four minutes to arrange and was arranged by a man who had never arranged anything in his life, out loud, in front of about nine men. **A foreman of fifty-one, in a shed, being asked, out loud, in a room she has to stand in, for the first time in nine years.** She answers in about four minutes, as arithmetic, with no speech in it: the bank is not mine, it is a thing nine families agreed to and it is not written down and cannot be produced, and that is why there is nobody to sue and that is why she can be the whole of it and is not a person anybody can be angry with; the form came with a heading over it and nothing under it and the person who filled it in filled in a thing, **and nobody has ever been asked what the words mean and there is no post in this empire whose job is to stand in front of a heading and ask; I have been on this bank since I was nine and I have never once in nine years been asked to read a word of anything, and a man in a city has now found out the rate on a wall and I have known the rate on this wall since I was seventeen.** A man of about thirty from the second gang says she could have told him, and she says she could have and did not and is not going to now, **because a person who is paid nothing can be asked, and a person who is paid nothing is also the only kind of person in this cut who can be told no by anybody. If I start telling, then in about four years there is a piece of paper in this shed with a sentence of mine on it and a day on it and a name at the foot of it, and I have watched a man of thirty-eight do exactly that with his mother's handwriting and I have read what it cost him, and I am not starting at fifty-one with a boy at home.** Then the other three things, flatly, in order: the second season began in the first month of this year, about the length a season is, about forty-one people, nobody agreed it and nobody refused it and nobody signed anything, wages held against a company whose whole money is four shillings a year and all of it the rent of a roof — **that is the cheapest hold there is and it is not a villain's hold and it is an ordinary hold, and it is cheap because there is nothing in it to break**; a factor's letter on a bench in the store up the road, four lines, nineteen days when it went on the bench and about forty now, wanting the name of a person who may be served and that person's consent, **and it is not going to be answered, and every day it is not answered is a day nobody has been entered, and I know what a day of that is worth and I am not going to be the one who spends it**; and about four people in that bay cannot read a paragraph, never asked once, not asked a second time, **and there is no column for them and there is not going to be one, and the reason there is not going to be one is that a column for them would be a column somebody would have to fill.** Then the sentence she has been carrying since the sixth month of last year: **A carriage is not a wage. A thing that pays for a person to come and does not pay them for the day is not an offer, it is a way of counting how many rooms a person is in. I said that in that bay in the sixth month of last year to about thirty people, and it was the truest thing anybody said that month, and it has not been useful to anybody since, because being right about a thing is not the same as a thing being done about.** Told she has said that before: **I have said it once, and I have not said it to this room, and there is a book-keeper on that bank who has wanted the other half of it since the third month and is not going to ask me for it, and the reason is that the day he asks is the day I stop saying the half I have got.** Told she has never given him the other half either: **no, and I am not going to, and it is not in this shed, and I have had it since the third month and I use it about four times a season, and it does not go into a room that writes it down, and you can put that in whatever you keep and I will not be asked about it again.** Nobody thanked her; she said in advance that nobody was to, and the man who arranged the asking did not thank her and said afterwards in the yard that he had wanted to and had been told not to and understood why. Then she got the box down off the shelf, because it was in her way, and there was nothing in the world that had ever told her to open it. She opened it: one side of a sheet of good paper, not printed, a seat's own device cut into the head, six numbered lines and a foot, the foot a seal and **nothing under the seal**, and the second line asking that no inference of any kind be drawn from the absence of any name, and **her name at the top of it. Not at the foot. At the top**, in the ordinary way, and nobody has ever asked her whether she wanted it there and there is no form anywhere in this empire that requires anybody to ask. The third line she read twice, the second reading slower, with the men who had not gone standing at the door: *where a recorded claim is entered and marked disputed, and the family of the claimant holds no enforceable asset, the person of that family who is of age and resident is the family's representative in this matter, is to be entered as such in the office of the seat, and no further consent of that person is required.* **That is me, and I am not a person of any family and I have never been in a book of anybody's, and that is because the job is not written down. A company of nine families and forty-one people is a family, and the only one of us with a place of custom or of work in a book is the one they cannot write down either. I have been that for about eleven years without knowing it, and I have been unfindable on purpose, and now I am findable by accident.** Asked who put her name on it: **nobody put my name on it. Somebody put a class on it and the class has got one member, and there is a woman in a city four hundred and thirty miles up this river who is a member of the same class, and she found out in the fourth week of the fifth month by a district clerk walking down a lane with a board, and she said no out loud in a room with a day on it, and it did not work.** She put the sheet down on the bench, face up, where anybody who came in could read it. **Nobody has been asked. There is no form anywhere in this empire for a person to be asked, and there is no form anywhere in this empire for a person to be let out of a thing that does not need them, and the word for what she has just been put into is the same word the mason's four days are under and the same word the wage of a dyer of twenty is under, and the word is *held*, and a person who is held cannot get up and go.** Halla Wray got up and went, and left the sheet on the bench, and shut the door, and it is a quarter past the seventh hour and the lamp is not lit.

## Chapter 199 — remainder of the entry

She is in four bays because a paper says so and the paper is not the reason; she is a party; **there are four of a certain kind of thing in her own rooms, one of them there since the first month of the year before last, and she has not thought about them in about two years and did not think about them tonight.** She said in advance, in a room, with the reason: **I am not going to decide anything in a room on a day when one of us has been up two nights.** Neither of them has been up two nights, it is the day, and **the sentence is the reason there is a scene here and not an apology.** He comes at about the seventh hour; she makes him sit; he says nothing for about a minute and a half. Then she puts a book on the table: **a hard-backed book with no office on it, hers, her own hand in it in about nine places**, opened near the back and turned round, with **two boxes ruled on the page in her own hand with a straight edge and a pen, and neither of them had ever been filled in.** It is not a form, not printed, never sent anywhere, not a copy of anything; she ruled it about eleven weeks ago because she could not get the sentence she wanted out of her head onto a page; she has not shown it to him and has not thought about it in about nine days. **Both of them are you. Not you the person. You the two instruments.** And the two instruments, kept apart in the order she has kept them apart for about a year: a **paper** — a court order made in a form in four days with a day on it, requiring her in any room he is in, producible by anybody holding it, produced four times, every one of them producing her in a room in front of about thirty people, the last in the fourth week of the sixth month, and she was in it and could not be examined and was not examinable and was in it anyway — and a **watch**, a separate instrument that does not name him and does not name her, requiring only that a person who is not from the office that cannot be named be in any room he is in. **It does not say who. There are about four hundred people in this city who could satisfy it, and it has been satisfied four times, and three of those four times it was me**, and the fourth was a girl of about twenty-five at an intake desk who opened a wrapper because she was going to open it anyway. So it is not about you and it is not about me and it is about a room — **and that is the part that has taken me about eleven weeks to write down: a person who is safe because nobody can instruct her and useful because somebody needs her standing in a room is a person with two boxes filled in on the same form.** She puts the pen down. **That is not a complaint. It is not a thank you. It is a description of a person**, she has been that person since the second month and has nobody to be it with, because everybody she knows is somebody's clerk; **it has taken me eleven weeks to work out that the sentence is about me and not about the arrangement, and about nine days to work out that I do not mind**; and she says both out loud because **my third line forbids advice and my fourth line says a fact is not advice, and I have said the words of both of those lines in this room tonight and I have never said them out loud in a room before.** He says there is a third thing; it has no box and there is no form anywhere in this empire that makes one and she has looked. He asks her to say it so he can write it down; she says no, and it is the first time anybody has refused him anything. **He tries four of them, out loud, standing up, in the way a man who has spent this spring learning to say a hard thing in a room tries a sentence and finds out whether it is true.** The first: that he could not do this without her — no, in four words. The second: that he would be careful — **that *careful* is a word for a person who is going to do a thing and it is not a thing and it goes in a book.** The third: that he had thought about it for about eleven weeks and could not make it into a sentence about the two of them — **the first of the four that was true, and it was not enough.** The fourth he does not finish. *You have stopped.* **I have stopped because I have got to the end of what I prepared and I have not prepared any more of it, and I am a man who prepares things, and there is nobody in this empire I would rather have stopped at the end of a sentence than in.** **Then that is the sentence. You have got to the end of what you prepared. That is what the third thing is, and it has no box because there is no form in this empire that will hold a sentence a man has not finished, and I am not going to help you finish it and I am not going to ask you to.** And: **that is not a thing anybody can be a party to.** No — **and that is the first good thing anybody has said in this matter since the first week of the fifth month, and I have written it on nothing.** And the shape of the third thing: **a thing this empire can put in a book is a thing this empire can ask about, and there is one thing in this room that is not going in a book** — not because it is unimportant, but because if it is written down then in about four years a clerk can hold it up and read it out and ask them both the same question, and a person remembering a thing and a person reading a thing back are two different acts and only one of them is evidence. Then the rule: **the rule of the twelfth month of last year is there, on the table, in both our heads; either may put a choice in writing with a day on it and the other must answer in a room, in words, with a date; it has been used six times, and it could be used tonight in about nine seconds and it would be the correct instrument, and it would be a document, and there would be a day on it, and a name at the foot of it, and about four years.** *Are you not going to use it.* **We are not going to use it and a correct non-use is as good as a use and it is entered as one.** Then she fills in the two boxes in her own hand with the day on it and reads them back twice, the second slower, and he does not read it, does not ask to, and does not touch the page. *Two names that are the same name.* **Two boxes and one name in each, and they are the same name, and that is the honest fact. It is why I ruled them by hand instead of writing one out and drawing a line under it**: a line under one name would have made it look like a single thing, and it is two things that happen to land on the same person four times since the second month. Then the other two sentences, because the page would be dishonest if she did not: **the undertaking of the fourth of the first month is live and has not been retired and has not been amended and has not been exercised tonight, and I am not going to exercise it tonight, and it stands**; and *I know. I have not put it in front of you once since the fourth month and that is not an oversight and I have thought about it every week.* *I do know why.* *Say it.* **Because there is nothing in it that I have got a reason against, and if I put it in front of you and you gave me a reason, then I would have to act on the reason, and the reason would be in a room, and I have watched about four hundred people in this city be careful in about four hundred rooms and I am not going to be the four hundred and first, and that is the whole of why I have not put it there and it is a bad reason and I know it is a bad reason.** It is a bad reason, **and it is the reason I have not asked you for it, and we are both hiding behind the same bad reason and neither of us is going to say so out loud, and that is the third thing and it has no box.** Then he says she is not going to say the price of it, and she says **I have said it ten times in a room and I am not going to say it an eleventh time in this one, and I am not saying it because it would put a number in a room and the number is the only part of me that anybody can be held to and I have had enough of being held to.** And that she has never once asked him for anything. And the one thing she is going to ask: **I am asking you not to thank me.** *I was not going to.* **I want it written down that I asked and that you said you were not going to, and that there is no form anywhere that requires it**, and there are four of them in her rooms and one has been there since the first month of the year before last and has not been sent and has not been opened, and she has not thought about it tonight and is not going to start. Then the lamp, and the door, and the two of them standing. **In the fourth week of the fourth month of this year he left at about the ninth hour and she shut the room, and that is written down, and it is not going to be unsaid, and he has not asked her about it and she has not offered.** **On the third day of the third week of the seventh month of the year after there is a lamp that is still lit at about the ninth hour and a door that is open and nobody shuts it**, and the difference between those two facts is the whole of what the last three weeks were for, and **there is no form anywhere in this empire that requires a door to be shut, and there is no column anywhere in this empire for the two boxes being filled, and the whole of what either of them has done tonight is the first thing either of them has done that no form in this empire requires.** It is not entered. **About four hundred and thirty miles of road is what it would cost anybody who wanted to come and see it, and there is no fund, and nobody is going to be told, and the reason is not privacy. It is that a fact said in a room with two people in it is the only kind of fact in this empire that is not evidence of anything, and they would rather have that.** And the thing left standing, in her mouth, not a comfort and not a reward and not about the hearing and not about the mark on his wrist and not about the day he lost: **The third thing has no box. That is what the whole of it since the second month has been for. I have been the woman with two boxes filled in on one form, and I have been the most dangerous person in this matter, and I have been the person nobody in it can protect, and those three things have been true at once since the second month of this year, and I did not know it and he did not know it, and it has now been said out loud in a room, and it is not going in either of our mouths again.** Nobody was thanked. Nothing was forgiven. **There is nothing in the room to forgive and she has said that in a room before and said it again tonight in different words**, and he did not say his mother was right, and nobody told him she was kind, and the day is a day and the door is open and nothing has come through it yet.

## Chapter 200 — remainder of the entry

**The second season was asked for on the fourth day of the last week of the seventh month, in a bay, standing up, by a man who said out loud before he started that he had been asked to ask and had not volunteered.** He has a factor's letter of four lines on a bench that has not been answered and is not going to be answered, and **about nineteen people who said no with a reason in the first season are still behind that bank, and this is the first week since the first month in which any of them has been asked anything at all.** The list of works was read out and **a man was first on it.** He had asked, on the fifteenth of the seventh month of a year that is not this one, to be asked last, and he was written first, and he has never corrected it, because a list of works is read out and a name at the top of it is the one everybody hears, and he wanted to hear it. **Kem Ostry is a carter.** He works the Slade to the mill wall and back with a cart of no particular description, has no employer of record, and has been paid at a guild rate of **one shilling and sixpence a day for a barrow of stone, a man and a boy**, since the fourth month, and his name has been in nobody's column anywhere. He said no at the gate in the first season in the same words as about sixty other people and gave a reason: **a name at the foot of a list of works means somebody comes and finds out whether it is working every fortnight for a season, and he had been the one watching since the fourth month and would like it stopped.** He was not argued with and not asked a second time; the reason was entered **because a person is entitled to have the reason they gave written down in the place where the no is, and not in a letter.** He was not asked a second time either, and **that is the finding: nobody had asked him, so nobody had to ask him again, and the reason nobody had asked him is that a person who is owed a wage cannot be asked about a season, because asking is a conversation and a conversation with a person whose wage is held is a conversation with a person who is losing money by having it.** *You have not asked me once since the first month, and I have been waiting for you to.* **Noted, and I did not ask you because I am not allowed to, and I am not allowed to because I am the one who put a roof in a box on the second day of the second week of the fourth month, and a man in an office read a definition out to a mason in a corridor in the third week into the fourth week of the third month, and both of those are the same sentence said by two people in four hundred and thirty miles, and I have carried mine since the second day of the second week of the fourth month without anybody to hand.** He said it again in the same words; about nine men in three gangs heard it; **the reason went in the book in the place where the no is.** Then the rest, one after the other, out loud, in a bay, in about four minutes, and **about nineteen people said no again.** Every one gave the reason they gave the first time, none of the nineteen reasons was the same as any other, and none was a reason about the weather or the mill or money; he wrote all nineteen in the third column of the bay's own ledger with the day on each, **and read the whole page back twice, and the second reading was slower and was the one he did not skip.** **Nobody in that bay has ever asked him where he got the double reading from, and he has been asked twice about it by a woman of thirty-four four hundred and thirty miles up this river and has not told her and is not going to be asked; the first reading is for the words and the second reading is for the people who did not have them the first time.** Then the nineteen reasons, read out, not the names, **because a name in a room is a thing that can be carried out of a room**: a boy on a list of works with a day against it and somebody coming to look at it every fortnight; *I am the one who holds the book and I will not hold a book that has a season in it*; *I have not been asked anything in nine years and I am not going to be asked in the first week*; *I said no once and the reason went in a book and nobody argued with me, and I would like that to happen twice*; *I am paid by a man who has not got four shillings a year and he would have to find it out of me*; *the last time there was water in it I was the one who went out on the low side with a rope and I am not doing it on a paper*. And the nineteenth, a woman with two children who had come down the lane in the rain, in four words: **I have to be home.** *That is not a reason.* **That is a reason. A person is entitled to have the reason they gave written down in the place where the no is, and not a second question, and not an opinion from the back of a bay. I have been keeping books since the second month of next year and I have read every form in this store and there is not one page in any of them that says a person has to give you a better reason than that one.** She went home at about the seventh hour; her name is in the third column with a day on it and no reason attached beyond the four words, **because the four words were the reason.** And: **a refusal is a thing a person is entitled to make once and have it kept and not be troubled about it again until the season comes round, and the season came round and about nineteen people said no again and they are still behind the bank.** About four people in that bay cannot read a paragraph: never asked once, not asked on the fourth day of the last week of the seventh month, no column and none going to be one, **because a column for them would be a column somebody would have to fill.** A girl of seventeen at the back on a written engagement at forty-five pence a day and four days a week doing the thing she is paid for; **not spoken to about the season, did not ask, and a person who is paid to read is a person who can be told to stop**, which he has said out loud in this bay three times and has not sent anybody to anybody and is not going to; not thanked, not going to start, **and nobody asked her to read anything that carries a rule, because there is no rule in that bay to carry and there has not been one since a man of fifty refused to rule a fourth column in a ledger in the second week of the eighth month of next year in about nine seconds.** Then the hold, read out: against the Slade Cut Company, being a thing, in the matter of a recorded claim entered and marked disputed, wages held at the same rate and for the same period, and a person whose wages are so held not to be paid that held sum by any person. **The guarantee, printed whole, word for word identical to Chapters 0161, 0164, 0170, 0180 and 0190, and no child named.** A bill at Lowcross of nineteen pounds three and fourpence, unpaid, nobody liable, about a penny a day the real cost, no line for a bridge in that fund and none in nineteen years, four hundred and eleven people on the far side, three hundred and eighty-eight crossings, **and those are two figures and they are never the same.** **The thirty-one of Mosswake are thirty-one, and there is a name and a door against every one of them in a returning officer's own hand, and there is no form anywhere in this empire for asking a returning officer what he wrote down after he had walked it.** The factor's letter of four lines on a bench since the second week of the third month, requiring the name, place of custom and description of a person who may be served together with that person's consent, saying that without both it cannot proceed and will not proceed and the matter will stand against the works until one is given — **it is not going to be answered, and every day it is not answered is a day nobody has been entered.** Nobody thanked anybody; nothing was forgiven, nothing paid back, nothing in that bay reversed. Then the last of it, four hundred and thirty miles up this river, in the plainest words and none of it a question. **The fourth counter of a notary's table, a sheet of printed matter with three ruled boxes on it, an oak slab and a rail and a fee board of four things and none of them time.** Box one: a name whose witness status was restored in the fourth week of the sixth month with a day on it and a name at the foot of the entry, on a roll, findable, with no way in this empire to put a name back a second time. Box two: a name entered as the representative of a family on the fourth day of the third week of the fifth month by a clerk filling a box the way the box is printed, and that person said no out loud in a room with the day on it and it did not work. **Two of them are filled with the names of two people who are not the clerk.** Box three is a description, and the description says **that there is a person in this matter who has not been asked and has not been found** — and it does not say a name, does not say a hand, does not say a lane or a county, and says two facts that are both findable and neither of which is a person. **There is a fourth space on that sheet with no heading over it, where a heading would go, and nobody has ever put one there, and about two hundred and forty people have never known it is there.** On the fourth shelf of a received room in that city there is a bundle marked *damaged, four items, uncollated*, there about twelve years, **not opened, not named, and it is a location and it is not a discovery**, and there is no form that requires a person to go and look at a bundle because they have found out that it is there. And the door of that room is a door and it is open and **nothing has come through it yet**, and a person may now ask for a thing in a room, and about four hundred and thirty miles of road is what that costs, and there is no fund. **The entry was read back twice and the second reading was slower. A girl of seventeen about four hundred and thirty miles down a river made that the rule of a bay in the second week of the fourth month, and it is now the rule of a counter, and nobody decided to make it the rule of a counter, and that is what a rule is.** There was one person in that room: a clerk of thirty-four, eleven years at that counter, not a lawyer, a party to an instrument about a dead woman's name, who has said the price of it out loud in a room ten times and nobody has thanked her once and would like it written down that she did not, **because in about four years somebody is going to read that she said the price ten times and they are going to assume somebody heard it.** Nobody had heard it. And at about the sixth hour, alone, at a counter with a rail on it, in a room with a pane out of the window, she said out loud, to nobody: **A person who is not told has not been erased. A person in a house that is shut has no address. Those are two different things and the first one is the harder one, and I have known the second one since the first month and I have never once been able to say it out loud without the first one coming with it, and I am saying both of them now and I am not going to leave one out to make the other one easier. This has been about the second one for about a hundred and forty years and it is about the first one since the first month, and a form printed in about nine hundred buildings has been asking the wrong person for about a hundred and forty years, and the only time it got asked at the right person it was in a kitchen with a pencil, and the person who asked it said the price out loud first.** Nobody thanked her. **And about nineteen people said no again, and they are still behind a bank, and about four hundred and thirty miles up this river a door is open and nothing has come through it yet.**

## What Volume 04 Batch 0005 resolved, what it did not, and what it hands on

**Resolved:** a hearing has a name and two dates, and both dates are in one minute book with a clerk's name at the foot of them, and one of the two is four years off. A form printed in about nine hundred buildings has been asked what it means, and the fifth line of it has been put in a room and read out and answered in nine minutes, and it is the only instrument in this empire that can hand a person the rest of what they wrote, and it has been used once. A court asked a clerk and a magistrate whether a woman of sixty-three is on a roll and she is not and cannot be. Pell Vey's hand is documented by comparison, his delivery stub is in a clerk's hand at the foot of an entry, his file is in four places and one of them is a back room, and he refused to answer in the same fourteen words on three days. A woman of twenty has found out in the ordinary way, from a board and a basket, that she has been entered as a representative, and has said no in a room with the day on it, and the no has not worked. A magistrate has given evidence about a river, named no person, and entered a finding against her own practice in a room with a witness in it, and it is not absolution and it is not a conversion. A man of thirty-eight has attempted a reconstruction out of a procedure, has failed, and has lost a day of his own memory that is named as a day and is not coming back. A name is restored to a roll with a day on it and a name at the foot of it. A question has been named for the first time anywhere in the manuscript and has not been answered and is not going to be. A foreman of fifty-one has been asked, out loud, in a shed, for the first time in nine years, and has answered in arithmetic. Two boxes on a form have been filled with the same name on the same night, and a third thing with no box has been said out loud in a room and has not been written down. And the second season has been asked for and about nineteen people have said no again.

**Not resolved, and not this batch's to resolve:** the act done by a person who was not heard is not undone and cannot be; nobody can prove whether a seat gave an order and the name that would prove it is not going to be said aloud; the question about a clause has never been answered in nine years by the court that asked it; a woman of sixty-three is not on any roll and cannot be put on one because she was never asked; nobody was entered that anybody entered was asked about first, and two people now are; a wage is held in a household of one and about forty-one wages are held against a roof and a mason's four days are held and a book-keeper's factor's letter is on a bench; a date four years off cannot be unscheduled; the forty-one forms have been checked and not thanked; the end of the run, the row, the striking-out, the column headed *by whose authority* and the name in it are still not read out; the Ashfall bundle is shut and unnamed and what Ilyra Kest found is unanswered; the two sealed pages are shut with two refusals between them and no third; and the volume's own next question — if the office was meant to be temporary, who changed it into an inheritable one, and why are dragons themselves bound by the altered compact — is not asked in this batch and is not going to be asked in the next one either.

# Volume 05, Chapters 201–210, Batch 0001

## Chapter 201 — remainder of the entry

**The head of a file is a printed label with four things on it and one of the four is a box: *Matter. Place. Day. The person, institution or thing engaged in the matter.*** The other three she can do standing up. She cannot fill the fourth, and she fills it with the thing the roll gave her, and the thing is a household, and the matter is a claim about a Kest house about four miles off the road with nobody in it. **A return is one printed sheet with that column on it, filled in at this end and sent out, and a keeper of a roll copies it into her own book, and that has been the ordinary way for about a hundred and forty years and there is no form that requires a keeper to be asked whether the column is right before she copies it.** She goes down the bundle and is not looking for anything on purpose: in about nine of the returns the column holds a household, in about four a company, in two a company of watermen, in one a chapel, and in one there is a line through the column where a name had been, struck out with a pen and not a rule. **Not one of them had a person in it, except one.** That one is dated the fourth day of the third week of the fifth month, for a district about four hundred and thirty miles up this river, and the column says a name, and she knows the name and has not said it out loud in that room and is not going to. **So the office asked a roll of households for a person, and a roll of households gave it one, and the office wrote the one down, and the office did everything correctly and the person said no and the no did not work.** Her own name is at the foot of that return, in her own hand, in about nine seconds, and she has not been asked and nobody has been asked, and she has thought about those nine seconds every week since the fifth month and has told nobody. **A form which cannot be asked cannot be un-filled, and it cannot be un-filled by the person who filled it.** That is the whole of what she has got out of nineteen years and a tray and it is not in any of the eleven books and she has looked. She writes *household* on the file head and the day and the place and the matter, and then she comes back to the fourth line and writes a second line under it, in the same hand, in about nine seconds: **I am asked to state the person, institution or thing engaged in the matter. There is no person engaged in it. There is a household of nine doors about four miles off the road, empty, and a claim, and a finding on a page. Nothing in this bundle is a person except one line, and the one line is a person of twenty about four hundred and thirty miles from this desk, and I did not choose her and I was not asked about her and I entered her in the fifth month, correctly, in the ordinary way, and my name is at the foot of that sheet and it is the second thing in this matter with my hand at the foot of it and the first one I did not choose.** Then the thing that has no form behind it: **a file with two lines under a box is a file that will be asked about, and in about four years a person with a copy of the right page can put it side by side with the return and ask a clerk of this office to explain the difference, and she will have to explain it, and she cannot.** So she says out loud to an empty room that there are two ways and the second one is hers: write the one line, put her name at the foot, carry the tray down the corridor and say nothing, and in four years there is a file with a household in it and a clerk in it and a day on it, **and that is the whole of the protection and it is worth fourpence of ink** — and the other way, which is that a person who writes the second line cannot be asked to produce the first one, because she will produce it gladly, and say out loud in whatever room she is in that she filled that box on the fourth day of the third week of the fifth month and that nobody asked her and that she did it correctly, in the same breath with the finding that the no did not work. **A clerk who takes a file out of the ordinary course has not done anything a form forbids, because no form in this empire has a line about it, and there is no form anywhere that requires a clerk to put a sheet in a press, and that is why it is hers to decide and why it is hers to be shown.** Then the corridor. An unnamed carrier who has carried trays for about nine years puts his hand on it and stops, because there are two lines on the head where there is one. **Who fills that one in — the person, institution or thing?
