Then she paid the day out of what she had, which was most of it and not all of it, and shut the door and stood in it with her coat on. There was a copy of nine lines in the coat and she did not take it out. **Nobody asked her anything that day and nobody asked her anything the next day, and there is no form anywhere in this empire for asking a woman of sixty-three the same question twice, and the first asking was in a doorway in the second month of the year after and it was done by a man who cuts kindling and who has told nobody since and is not going to be asked and does not want to be.** And last: **the date four years off that is in a minute book in a building about nine miles from a counter in this city is about her and she does not know it exists, and there is nobody who could tell her, and she is going to go and stand in a room where boxes are filled in and she is going to find out nothing about that either, and she has decided to go anyway, and that is the whole of what she has decided.**

## Chapter 209 — remainder of the entry

She has a sheet of her own with a heading she wrote herself and one day on it and she has told nobody about either. She has been in this city since the third week of the fifth month, and has been to four offices and two counters and one hearing room with a rail in it, **and after the fourth office she stopped going to offices.** A woman of about fifty-five is at a table, and she is not a lawyer and she is not a party and she is not a judge, and she has never been asked what any of it meant. She stood in the doorway for about a minute and a half before she said anything, **because she had come about four miles across this city to that door and she had rehearsed it the whole way and about a third of it had gone out of her head in the last stretch.** *You filled a box.* **I did.** **On the fourth day of the third week of the fifth month of this year. And the box was for the person, institution or thing engaged in the matter. And you wrote a name in it, and the name was mine, and I did not know there was a box and I did not know there was a day, and about four hundred and thirty miles up this river a woman of about sixty walked down a lane with a sheet on a board and read it off a roll and told me, and I said no out loud in a room and it did not work.** *Yes. All of that is correct. Sit down if you want to sit down.* **I will not sit down. I have come about four miles to ask a question and I am going to ask it standing up and I am going to be standing up when you answer it, and if I sit down I will be a person being received and I have been received by a great many people this year and I have not been asked one question by any of them.** Then the question, and it is the third time in about a hundred and forty years that one person in this empire had asked another person what a word on a form meant, and the first time anybody had asked on purpose. **How did the box get filled. Not what it says. Not what a representative is, a clerk of thirty-four told me that at a counter in the second week of the ninth month and gave it to me for nothing and would not write it down, and I have it. I want the other half.** I want to know how a person of twenty about four hundred and thirty miles away got into a box in a building she has never been in, by a hand she has never seen, out of a book she has never had, and I want it in a room and out loud, and I have been about six weeks working out that there is exactly one person in this empire who can tell me and she is about nine miles from the counter I stood at in the ninth month. Orla Denning put her pen down and answered it in about four minutes, standing on the other side of a table with her hands flat on it, and she did not sit either. **A return. A return is one printed sheet with a column on it and the column is headed for the person, institution or thing engaged in the matter, and it goes out to a district, and the district keeps a roll of households, and a keeper of a roll of households copies the column into her own book where a person can see it. The column is not filled in by the district. The column is filled in here, in this room, at this end, in advance, out of a roll, because a keeper cannot invent a person and cannot be asked what a word on a form means.** When the office asks a district a question the district has no answer to, it gets back the only answer there is, and writes the answer down in a box with a day on it, and sends it, and the keeper copies it into a roll. **The question the office asked your district was: the person of that family who is of age and resident. A roll of households does not say a person is of age and resident. It says a household is at a door. So what came back was a household, and the household that came back was yours, and the entry for it says a place of custom or of work and it does not say which of the women in it, and there is no box in this empire for the difference between a person and a trade.** *And you took the household, and you put a person in it.* **I took what came back and I put it in a box that takes a thing as well as a person, and I did it in about nine seconds, and I was not asked, and nobody was asked, and I have written on the head of a file in this room that I did it and that nobody asked me and that I did it correctly, and the file is not in the press, and there is no form anywhere in this empire that requires a clerk to put a file in a press.** *Why is the file not in the press.* **Because a file in the press is a file that a person can be told about, and being told about it is a service and being served is a party, and there is no way back out of a party. And I have been a clerk in this room for nineteen years and I was not in a party before the fifth month of this year and I would like to stay out of one for as long as this building lets me, and that is a reason and it is not a good one and I have not improved it.** Then the question she had come four miles for, which she had not said yet. *How many of them are there.* **I cannot tell you.** *You are the office. You are the only office in this empire that does this. You must be able to tell me.* **I have kept eleven minute books for nineteen years and I have entered about four thousand things into them and every one of them is a thing a room decided, and a thing a room decided does not record the thing that was done to a person in about nine seconds in a box. The office keeps no register of them. That is not carelessness.** A register is a book of persons and this office has never kept a book of persons, and it never needed one, because the persons were in other districts' books and this office never had to look at them. **So the number is not lost. The number has never been anywhere.** It is in a roll of households in every district in this empire, and every one of them is about four hundred and thirty miles from this desk, and not one of them has a keeper in it who has been asked what the fourth column is for. And I would say to you, as one clerk to another, and I have never said anything of the kind to anybody in nineteen years, that there are two of you that I know of and that is not a number, it is a memory, **and a memory of two is worse than a figure of none, because a figure of none is a thing a man can look for and never find, and a memory of two is a clerk of fifty-five in a room with a pane out of the window.** Then the price of the next four minutes, and the price is the truth and it is not about her. **You have just asked a question about a form in a room, out loud, and you are twenty, and you are not a party to anything, and nobody is going to serve you with this, and there is no instrument anywhere in this empire that will let anybody tell you what I am about to say you cannot have. A person who asks becomes answerable for the answer. Not you. Me.** In about four years a man with a copy of the right page can put this room side by side with a sheet of my hand and ask a clerk of this office why the column was filled in at this end in advance, and she will have to answer it, and her name will be at the foot of it, and the answer will be that she was not asked in about nine seconds in the fifth month of this year, and there is no form anywhere that asks her whether she was asked. **You have moved a thing from a book with no name on it to a room with two people in it, and that is the whole of what you have done,** and the two people are a woman of fifty-five who has written nineteen years of minutes and a woman of twenty who cannot read a paragraph and has been in this city since the third week of the fifth month, and neither of us can be protected and you have not gained anything at all except the four minutes. *I know that. I priced it in the fourth week of the fifth month to a woman of about sixty holding a board and she wrote it in a column and there was no box for a why and I have looked. I came four miles to be answerable for a question. That is the first thing anybody has ever let me be the price of and I am not going to be coy about it in a room.* Then she did not hand over the sheet. *You want to see it, because a day is the only part of it anybody can find, and you have a day, and you have been holding it since the fifth month, and you have not told me about it and I am not asking.* **You are not asking.** **A person who is not asked has not been given a duty, and I have known that since about the seventh month of next year and I have never been on the wrong side of it until this year.** *Keep it. I am not giving it to anybody. It is a sheet of my own with a heading I wrote myself and there is one day on it and the day is the only part of the whole business that can be found, and the minute I give it to an office it becomes a record of a person in an office, and I have spent three years refusing to make records of people and I am not starting with myself.* Then what she had come to say, and it is a decision, and it is hers. **I cannot get out of the box. You have not told me I can and I did not think anybody could, and I have asked four offices and two counters and a woman at a hearing and not one of them said the word *out* once. And I am twenty, and I am about four hundred and thirty miles from a lane with eleven doors on it, and my wage is held, and the only person who can tell me what the fourth column of that roll is for is a woman of about sixty who walks it once a year with a board and a basket, and nobody is going four hundred and thirty miles to ask her and there is no fund and no form for a fund, and I have said I was not going to add it up and I am not going to now.** **So here is what I have decided and it is the only decision I have and I have had it since the ninth month. I cannot get out of the box, and I am going to be the person who knows how it was filled, and I am not going to be a person who knows it alone.** In this city there is a counter where a clerk of thirty-four told me what a representative is and would not write it down. Four hundred and thirty miles up this river there is a lane with a roll of households on it. About nine miles from here there is a man with a book and no office on it and eleven questions in it and a twelfth line he has left empty because there is no post in this empire whose job is to ask. **That is three, and there is a fourth, and she is a clerk of thirty-eight at a window on a flood terrace about four hundred and thirty miles down this river, and she has never been asked anything either, and I am going to be asking four people instead of one**, and there is no form for that and nobody is going to help me and I am not going to be thanked for it, because there is no form anywhere that requires a person to be thanked and I have said that out loud too many times this year. Orla Denning did not write any of it down, and she said so. **I am not going to write that down either, and I want to be exact about why, because you will think it is kindness and it is not. It is that a woman of fifty-five who writes a woman's plan in her own book in about nine seconds has done to you the identical thing that was done to you in the fifth month of this year, and I have had the two of them side by side since about the fourth week of the ninth month and I have not been able to put them down again.** **The thing that happened to you was a document. If I write your plan down then you have been got at twice by two people who were not villains, and one of them is standing in front of you on the other side of a table.** *That is the answer to the question I have not asked you yet.* *What question.* **Whether you are frightened of having written the two lines on that file head. You are. And it has cost you about four hundred and thirty miles of nothing and a tray, and you did it anyway, on a day, in a room, with nobody in it, and I have not got a word for what that is and I am not going to make one up at the end of a four-mile walk.** **I have a word for it, and the word is *expensive*, and you are twenty and you have not got the four years I have got, and I would like you to go and be asked four questions and not one of them here.** **Sit down if you want to sit down. The room is not going anywhere and neither am I, and I have a few minutes before the tray comes down this corridor, and there is a chair on that side with nothing on it.**

## Chapter 210 — remainder of the entry

**She can read and nobody at the district works has ever once in nine years asked her to read anything, and in the fourth week of the ninth month of this year she read six numbered lines out loud to nine men in that shed because she had decided to and nobody asked her, and it is the first time in her life that anybody in that shed has heard her read anything.** There is one sheet of her own hand in a book of her own, with the day against it; it is the words of a document and not the document, and **it has no name in it**, and she put it there on purpose, and she has read it since and has not added her name and is not going to; and the notice it was copied from is on the bench and has been since the seventh month, so the two of them are two sheets and not one. The second season came into its last weeks, the water was at the third-of-the-way mark, and about four hundred and forty foot of bank stood behind it with about nine men on it who had come because a word had been put in the yard and not because anybody had asked them. She did the ordinary things, **because a foreman who stops doing them is a foreman who has begun to make a speech of her own job**: the count of the works, which takes about four minutes; the water; the hold, which is against a company whose whole money is four shillings a year and all of it the rent of a building, **and which has not moved since the fourth week of the fourth month and is not going to move because a foreman is fifty-one and has a boy at home**; the factor's letter on a bench up the road, four lines, wanting a person who may be served and that person's consent, **and it is not going to be answered, and every day it is not answered is a day nobody has been entered.** Then the nineteen reasons, in a bay, in about four minutes, and not the names, the second reading slower. **They said it in the seventh month and they have said nothing since, and that is correct, and nobody is going to ask them again until the season comes round. A person is entitled to make a refusal once and have it kept and not be troubled about it again, and there is no form anywhere in this empire that entitles anybody to be troubled twice.** Then the four, in the open, standing, with the men looking at her, **none of them understanding that this is the fourth time in a year she has said it and the first time she has said it in a room with nobody to answer her**: **About four people on this cut cannot read a paragraph. They have never been asked once. Not in the first season, not on the fourth day of the seventh month, not in the second week of the tenth month, and not today, and there is no column for them and there is not going to be one, and the reason there is not going to be one is that a column for them would be a column somebody would have to fill.** And, crediting him and not taking it: **A book-keeper on this bank worked out the one way of reaching them in the second week of the tenth month and wrote down that he was not going to do it, with the day on it, and read it back twice, and he did it because a person who is paid to read is a person who can be told to stop. That is correct and it is the whole of the argument and I am not going to improve on it and I am not going to say it as though I thought of it.** And the other half, which is hers, and which she has been carrying since the third week of the seventh month and uses about four times a season: **I have been the reason I could not be found for forty-two years. Forty-two years on this bank and nine years holding the only job on it that is written down nowhere, and a child of nine did not choose any of it, and a foreman of fifty-one is in a box because of it. And I would like to be found.** I said that in this shed in the fourth week of the ninth month and some of you heard it and not one of you wrote it down, and that was correct, and there was no paper and nobody thought to fetch any. **So we have a woman on this bank who wants to be found, and four people on this bank who have never been asked anything, and one of those four is about the same age as the years I have been unfindable, and that is not a coincidence and I have not got a speech for it and I am not going to make one.** Then the question the whole of it turns on, asked out loud, standing up, in front of them, with it said in advance that nobody is going to answer it: **Who asks.** **That is the whole of the question and it has been in me since the ninth month and I have not said it in a shed because a shed is not a room that wants a record and I have spent nine years in rooms that do not want anything.** **So here it is in a room. A woman of twenty in a furnace town got put in a box in the fifth month and I got put in a box in the seventh month, and neither of us was asked, and there is no form in this empire for asking either of us, and the only instrument anybody has ever found for saying no to a box is a refusal to be served, and it did not work on the woman of twenty and it cannot work on me at all, because I was not served anything, I was entered by a class, and a class is not served on anybody, and a person who has not been served cannot refuse to be served.** That is the fifth time the protection and the catch have been the same act and I am not going to list them and I am not going to count them again, and nobody chose it, **and the reason nobody chose it is that the fifth time it happened it happened in this shed to me, and the four people it is about are not in this shed and have never been in a room together either.** **Now. There is no post in this empire whose job is to ask. I have been told by two people in the last six weeks that it has now been done three times about forms, and that two of the three were accidents and nobody chose either of them.** One was a room that wanted a record. One was a man who was frightened about his own household. And one was a woman of twenty who walked about four miles and stood at a desk and asked how the box got filled, and that one was on purpose, and nobody sent her, and I have not been told who she asked. **So three, and no post, and no plan, and I have asked nine men in a shed in the last two minutes and not one of them has said a word, and I did not expect one and I want it entered that I said in advance that I did not expect one, because a woman who asks a question in a room and then says afterwards that she expected nothing is a woman who has been in a room before.** And what she has got, and it is not a speech and it is not in a book: **Somebody asks, or nobody asks. And I do not know which, and neither do the three of them, and the only thing I have found out this year is that it happens to people who were not going to do it.** It happened to a room that wanted a record. It happened to a man who was afraid about his own household. It happened to a woman who was twenty and had come four miles. **There is no post. And that is the whole of the answer, and I have been waiting since the first week of the seventh month for somebody to stand in this shed and tell me what happens instead, and nobody is going to, and I am not going to ask them twice.** The shed stays quiet for about a minute and a half and then a man of about thirty from the second gang: **So you want one of us to do it.** **No. I do not want one of you to do it and if one of you offers I am going to refuse you, and I am telling you that now, in a room, in advance, so that none of you is a hero about it in about four years.** Here is my reason and it is the reason I have had since the fourth week of the ninth month and it is not a good reason and I know it is not a good reason and I am saying it anyway. **The moment one of you asks those four people, they are found. Not by the district and not by a seat. Found.** By whoever comes asking next, and there is always somebody who comes asking next, and I have watched a woman in a furnace town get found by a box and a day and a hand in about nine seconds by a person who was not a villain. **And I have been the one that could not be found, for forty-two years, and I have said in this shed that I would like to be found, and I have meant it, and I am telling you both of those things in the same breath and the second one is the one that decides it. So I am not going to be the reason four other people are found. If the four stay unasked this season then that is a decision and it is mine and I have made it and I am not going to be talked out of it by a man in a shed who thinks he has found something to do.** She put the book with the two sheets in it on the bench and left it there, and it stayed there all night, and in the morning the shed was open and about nine men came in and looked at it and none of them asked about it and none of them will. Then, at about the sixth hour on the second day, in the middle of it: a man of about fifty-five who has been on that cut for about thirty-three years, which is nine years less than she has, and who came the year after her and has never once said so, **and he is one of the four.** Halla Wray knew which one of the four he was before he was through the door, **and she knew it in the ordinary way a foreman knows the face of a man on her own bank, and there is no form anywhere in this empire in which a foreman's memory of a face is a record, and there never has been, and she has relied on that for nine years.** *Can you look at my drum. The chain has gone stiff and it wants oiling and I have brought it up here because the smith is at Lowcross and Lowcross is a bridge and the bridge is shut and I am not going over it for a chain.* **The chain wants the third link out and a drop of something in the bush. That is the whole of it and you could do it yourself with a nail.** *I have not got a nail.* That was the whole of it. It took about two minutes and he stood at the bench with the drum on it and she told him about the third link and about the oil, **and every man in that shed watched a foreman do a perfectly ordinary thing to a man who cannot read a paragraph. And she did not ask him.** Not about the hold. Not about the season. Not about the nineteen reasons or the two sheets of paper with a seal and nothing under the seal lying on the bench where anybody who came in could read them. She had asked the room in the last two minutes whether they knew who fills in a box, and she had said in advance that nobody was going to answer, and she had meant it as a kind of honesty, **and a man of fifty-five walked in about two minutes later who is one of the four people it is about and she did not ask him one word.** She thought about it for the rest of that week and has not got a better account of it than that and has not told it to anybody. **He would have said yes.** That is the part she cannot get round, and she is a woman who has been right about the rate on her own wall since she was seventeen and she is not a woman who is comfortable with being right. **He would have said yes, in a shed, out loud, in about two seconds, and then he would have been a man who had been asked, and a man who has been asked can be asked again by whoever comes next, and she would have started it. And that is not a reason, and she knows it is not a reason, and she gave it out loud in a shed in the second week of the eleventh month to a room as though it were.** **It is a bad reason. I have said in this shed that I would rather be asked than not, and about the bank I have been asked five times and said no five times and about this I have not been asked once, and the reason is that nobody has thought of it, and I have thought of it, and I have not asked, and that is the whole of what I have done in a year of standing in rooms. I would like somebody to put that on a slate and read it back to me**, and there is nobody here who is going to, and I am not going to ask one of the four men in this shed to do it either, for the reason I have just given, and I know exactly how that sounds. Then the water went down about a foot before the end of it and the second season came to its end with about nineteen people still behind that bank, and about four of them in that bay never having been asked anything, and a guarantee under the fire-and-water device standing offered and unanswered with about sixty children under sixteen inside it, and a bill at Lowcross unpaid and nobody liable and nobody paying it in a shed either, and a factor's letter on a bench wanting a person who may be served. **Nobody sent anybody anywhere.** The lamp went on at about the seventh hour, which is later than it ought to be, and the door of the shed was open, and **there is no post in this empire whose job is to ask, and three accidents are not a plan, and the woman who has the most reason in the world to be asked about a thing has still not been asked, and a foreman of fifty-one has decided, out loud, in a room, in front of about nine men, that she is not going to be the reason four other people are found, and that is the end of the season and it is not the end of anything else.**

## What Volume 05 Batch 0001 resolved, what it did not, and what it hands on

**Resolved:** two people are shown in a box and neither was asked, and the ordinary way each of them finds out is on the page in a clerk's own hands — a woman of twenty from a counter, a woman of twenty from a board and a basket, a foreman of fifty-one from a shelf in a shed. A clerk of about fifty-five has discovered that the entry in the box is in her own hand and that she was not a villain and was not asked, and has entered it, with the day, on a file head, and has taken the file out of the press and it is not going back. The rule this volume turns on is stated on the page in three pieces by three people and is not compressed anywhere: **a form which cannot be asked cannot be un-filled, and it cannot be un-filled by the person who filled it; the only thing standing between a person in a box and the rest of their life is somebody asking a question out loud in a room, and there is no post in this empire whose job is to ask; and a person who has not been served cannot refuse to be served, so the only instrument there is is no use at all to a person entered by a class.** The reason the machinery got the foreman is said in her own mouth and it is the fifth time the protection and the catch have been the same act and nobody chose it. A clerk of thirty-four has given the answer to what a representative entry is, out loud, at a counter, for nothing, and has not written it down, and the reason there is nowhere to put it is that there is no form in this empire for a document about a person who did not ask to be in one. A clerk of thirty-eight has answered a question about the word *resident* at a window and has said the price out loud before she answered, and the finding is that what goes in the box is whatever the roll has, and a roll of households has a household in it. A book-keeper has worked out the one way of reaching four people who cannot read a paragraph and has not used it and has entered the not-doing with the day on it and read it back twice. A woman of twenty-four has exercised the undertaking of the fourth of the first month for the fifth time, has refused to write down a sentence a man has not finished, has said the price of a party out loud ten times and been thanked zero times, and has said in advance that she will say no if anybody ever asks her to go and ask a question in a room on that bank. A woman of sixty-three has worked out that being unfindable is the reason she was never asked and not the reason she was safe, and has decided to go and stand in a room where boxes are filled in, on her own account, and nobody sent her. And a foreman of fifty-one has asked the room the question the batch exists to ask, out loud, and has not got an answer out of nine men, and has decided that she is not going to be the reason four other people are found, and has said the reason out loud and then said that the reason is a bad one.

**Not resolved, and not this batch's to resolve:** nobody is going to say who is going to ask on purpose; the woman of about sixty who keeps the roll of the district about four hundred and thirty miles up the river is unnamed and unasked and there is no fund and no form for a fund; the class cannot be counted by anybody and two of its members are known; the two who are in the boxes stay in the boxes; the act done by a person who was not heard is not undone and cannot be; the date four years off is in a minute book and cannot be unscheduled and the woman it is about does not know it exists; the guarantee stands offered and unanswered with about sixty children under sixteen inside it and no child is named; the bill at Lowcross stands at nineteen pounds three and fourpence, unpaid, nobody liable, no line for a bridge in that fund in nineteen years; the thirty-one of Mosswake are thirty-one; about four people in a bay who cannot read a paragraph have still never been asked once and a reader of seventeen has still not been thanked and is not going to start; the two sealed pages are shut with two refusals between them and no third; the Ashfall bundle is shut and unnamed; and the volume's own question — if the office was meant to be temporary, who changed it into an inheritable one, and why are dragons themselves bound by the altered compact — is not asked in any of the ten chapters and is not going to be.

## Chapter 211 — remainder of the entry

Then a man of about fifty-five, **one of the about four people in that bay who cannot read a paragraph, and who Rennick Adley has known and never said a word about**, comes to the end of the bench at about the ninth hour with a printed district slip. It has a heading, a day under it, four lines, a line for a name and a space for an officer's hand, and the heading says the water at four houses is to be shut a day and a night for cleaning a main, and any house without stored water will be classed as a complaint, and a complaint has to be made in writing by the person of the house, and the district cannot do it for anybody. **And there is no box on it anywhere, and the person of the house is a person and not a household and not a trade, and somebody wrote that down on purpose, which is more than a form has ever done, and the man cannot read a word of it and did not know it was a favour.** *I have got the whole of it in my hand and I cannot tell whether the four is mine or the next one along, and I am not going to put my name under it until somebody has told me what is on it.* **He could have said no.** He thought about it in about four seconds and the reason is that **a man who has been read to in a bay is a man who has been seen**, and a book-keeper who reads a slip out loud in a bay in front of nine men is doing to four men at once what a foreman of fifty-one refused in the second week of the eleventh month to do to one; so he walks out of the yard with the man, reads the heading first, then the four lines, in about a minute and a half, and is stopped twice, both times at a figure, both times to have it said again more slowly. **The line for a name and a man who cannot sign: I can put it down and you can make a mark under it, and I will read the mark back to you so that you know which one it is, and I will not write anything else and there will be no book.** A mark is made with chalk off the yard wall and read back twice, the second slower. *You are not going to write it down.* **Neither of them is going anywhere and that is the correct arrangement and I would like it noticed that I worked it out in about four seconds and that the four seconds were about you and not about the works.** Then the finding, said to the man and not to the two other men: **Nobody asked anybody what a word on a form means, and I want that said, and I want it said by me and not by a clerk, and I am the wrong man to say it and I am saying it anyway. A man came to the end of my bench with a slip in his hand and asked me to read him his own paper. That is not a question about a word on a form. That is a man asking a man to read him a thing that is his. It is not one of the three and I am not going to let it be counted as one of the three, because a count of what has been asked about forms is a count of people and that is the only thing that count is.** And: **it is the only one of the three that has ever been done, and it is the only one of the three that has cost nobody a thing.** Then the whole of the hold and the guarantee standing offered and unanswered on four hundred and forty foot of it with about sixty children under sixteen inside it, and the bill at Lowcross at nineteen pounds three and fourpence that nobody is liable for and no line for a bridge in that fund in nineteen years, **and none of that can be given to four men who cannot read a paragraph, and the finding of the second week of the tenth month stands and is not said again and not improved.** And: **A thing that is written down does not need anybody to want you found. It only needs somebody to go looking.** Then the decision, read back twice before he said it: **I am going to do it again, and I am not going to write it down and I am not going to tell the foreman, and there are three things in that and I will take them one at a time.** Not writing it down, because in about four years a man with a copy of the right page can come into that building and ask which men he read to and on what days and in what words, and the man will have a day against his name on a page in a book that keeps the Company's works, **and that is how a person who cannot read a paragraph gets found and nobody has to mean it.** Not telling the foreman: **A foreman who knew would be a foreman somebody could ask** — if she knows, she is a person on that bank who knows a thing about four men in that bay, and there is no form anywhere in this empire she could be asked under, and in about four years somebody would ask her. And the third, which he is worst at: **a thing that is written down is the only thing anybody can be held to and a thing that is not written down is the only thing that costs nobody anything, and the whole of this matter from the ninth month of this year has been a thing that was written down, and the instrument that gets to a person without costing anybody is the one that leaves no mark, and there is no form anywhere in this empire for a thing that leaves no mark, and I am not going to try to invent one.** Then the man comes back into the bay and asks whether he is one of the nineteen. It takes about four minutes because the names are in a different book, about nine hundred and forty of them in nine hands over about sixty years. He is not, and he never has been, because **you have never refused anything, because nobody in about thirty-three years has asked you whether you would, and a person who has not been asked has not said no and has not said yes and is not on either list.** And: **a man who has not been asked is a third thing and it has no word and it is the only one of the three that nobody in this matter has ever been in.** And: **I have just read a name out of a book to a man in order to tell him what he was not, and that is the one thing I have done today that I would not be able to do again, and I have not got a rule for it and I am not going to make one up at the end of a morning.**

## Chapter 212 — remainder of the entry

She stands in the doorway about four minutes and counts the trays: two up and one down. Then she goes in. The room is on the ground floor with a window about two feet wide and no pane in it, eleven books on a shelf, a table, and a woman of about fifty-five who did not get up. Two other people: a man of about thirty with a coat on by the door and a man of about sixty at the shelves. *Sit down if you want to sit down.* **I have come to stand.** The clerk puts her pen down, which she does about twice a year, and the two questions are ordinary and are not about a form: *Are you served.* No. *Are you in a matter.* No. **Then the third question, which is a name.** *She had heard a person say a name in that room. Not a clerk, not a party, not a return, not a book, not a hand at the foot of anything. A person, out loud, in a room, and it was not copied out of anything, and it was not written down, and it was not the second time in nineteen years because it was the first time in nineteen years.* Bess Tarrant says it in about two seconds and does not say it twice and does not spell it. The clerk says thank you and stops, because there is no form anywhere in this empire that requires a clerk to say thank you at a table. Then the old man at the shelves asks whether she is the one for the water; she says she has not come about the water; he says he has wasted her morning and goes, and does not apologise, and means it. **That is the whole of the room. Three people and a shelf of books and a tray going up a corridor.** Then *What do you want*, and Bess Tarrant tells her in nine sentences, standing: **I have come to stand in a room where boxes are filled in. That is the whole of the plan. I have read in my own coat a thing I cannot read, and I have had it read to me once in a doorway by a man who cuts kindling, and I have worked out since that asking works on a person and has never once worked on a piece of paper, and a form cannot be asked at all.** And the finding she came to deliver, to a woman and not to a room: **the machinery does not need me, it needs a person who writes, and you are that person, and you have written nineteen years of minutes and about four thousand things into them and nobody has ever once asked you what a box on a form is for, including me, until about four minutes ago, and I am sixty-three and I have come nine miles to say a thing to a person instead of to a building, and I would like it noticed that I did not write it down, and I cannot, and that is why I am standing here and not sitting down.** Then the decision, announced before it is made and unasked for: the clerk gets up off the chair, takes down one of the eleven books, does not open it, and puts it back — **that is a record of what a room decided, it is not a register of people, and this office has never kept one and never needed one, because the people are in other districts' books and we never had to look at them.** And: **A person who has been in a box is in a roll of households in a district about four hundred and thirty miles from this table, and it is in nobody's book in this building, and I could go and look at about nine hundred districts' rolls tomorrow and there is nothing whatever to stop me, and I have a book of about nine hundred and forty names on a shelf behind you that I could do it out of in about four days.** *Then do it.* **No. I am not going to, and that is the decision I made at about the third minute of this and I have not been asked for it by anybody, and I would like it entered that a person of sixty-three who cannot read a paragraph asked me for the one thing that would find her and I said no.** The reason takes about a minute and a half and is not a good reason and the woman who gives it says so: **a name in a book does not go looking, and a name in a book only goes looking when somebody goes looking, and I am the person in this building who goes looking, and if I go looking then I am doing to a woman who came on her own account the identical thing that was done to a woman of twenty in the fifth month of this year, in a box, in about nine seconds, by a person who was not a villain and who is sitting at this table — and you cannot read a paragraph, I would have to read you what I found, I would choose the words, and that is what happened to you in the ninth month and it would happen again in the first week of the twelfth, and the difference is that the second time it would be me.** Then the copy of nine lines comes out of the coat and is held out and is not taken. **That is yours. I am not giving it to you.** *I know you would have, and that is the difference between a man who cuts kindling and you, and it took me the whole of the second month to be able to say it out loud. He did not ask to keep it. You asked.* And then, last: **There is a day entered in one of the eleven books on that shelf, in the first week of the fifth month of this year, with my name at the foot of the line, and it is about four years off, and I have found out nothing whatever about it, and I am not going to find out anything about it, and I have thought about it on the walk here and I have decided that if I had found out about it I would not be able to do the thing I came here to do, and I would rather not know and stand in a room for nothing.** The clerk says she entered that day and did not know what she was entering and Bess Tarrant is the only person in this empire who could tell her and has just said she is not going to. *That is right.* **And nobody asked her one word about it, and there is no form anywhere in this empire that requires a person to be told a thing they have asked not to be told, and about four minutes later she was out in the street with her coat done up, and she stood at the end of it for about an hour and a half the way she stands at a wall most days, and the day was going, and she did not go back to the room above a shop until the light had gone off the wall.**

## Chapter 213 — remainder of the entry

**He came back.** He is about thirty-four; in the first week of the tenth month he asked what the word *resident* means on a return because he had moved in the second week of the ninth month from a street with a shop on it to rooms over a yard, four streets off, inside the same district, and he had a wife and a boy, and he wanted to know whether a word was going to be used about them. **He had said afterwards that he would rather not have asked, and she had said that that was the correct answer, and there is no form anywhere in this empire that requires anybody to be told anything after that. He did not ask her anything.** He puts down a slip with a reference on it, gives four words, pays fourpence for a copy made on a sheet of press in about a minute and a half, read out, agreed. That is the whole of the transaction: about four hundred of those a year and not one of them a question. Then he does not go. *I have got about four minutes and I would like to use them.* **What did you come back for.** *You did not answer my question. Not the word. The other thing you said first.* **I always say it first: a person who asks a question about a form in a room becomes a person somebody has answered, and there is no instrument anywhere in this empire that takes that back off him.** *I did not hear it. I heard a clerk being careful.* And then: **Nobody had told him and nobody was going to. The only reason he knew was that he had asked her to say it again in a room, and that is a thing a person can do, and it is the only thing anybody can do about it, and there is no form anywhere in this empire for asking a person to tell you what you have become.** She says it again, in about a minute, the same words, and the second time is the one that counts and no form says a thing counts twice. *It is worse than I thought.* It is exactly what he thought. Then he asks whether anybody had found him — **a plain question, in a room, out loud, and it is on the page because she has never had one of those at that window either, and the count of those is a different count and is not three and is not printed here.** And the count, stated: **You have, and there is no undoing it and no form anywhere in this empire to undo it with. And you are the second of the three. The first was a room that wanted a record, in the fifth month, in a court. The third is a woman of twenty who walked about four miles in the second week of the eleventh month and asked a clerk to explain how the box got filled, and she was the first one anybody did on purpose, and nobody sent her. Three, and none of the three of you is a plan.** Then, unasked, the thing he actually came for: **You moved in the second week of the ninth month, four streets off, inside the same district, and you did not tell anybody, and the reason is that there is nothing at this counter that asks you, and that is not a complaint, it is the shape of the building.** Your household is at a door four streets off and was at a door on a street with a shop on it, both inside this district, the roll has both, **and the roll does not say which people live at either door. It says a household is at a door.** And: **what came back to the office about your street, in the fifth month of this year, was a household. The box wanted a person, a household is what the roll had, and that is the whole of what has happened to you and your wife and your boy, and nothing has been done to you at all.** And *resident* means the roll at a door; not living in it, not paying anything, not anybody having agreed to anything; a man can be resident on four rolls in four districts and be on none of them in the sense he means; **you have been on one of them since the second week of the ninth month and you moved four streets off and the roll never noticed and the water did not care and nobody in this building ever knew there was anything to notice.** *That is what I was afraid of.* **That is the opposite of what you were afraid of, and the answer you got was an ordinary answer and it cost you the only thing you have.** Then the basket under the sill, about nine inches deep, off a form that calls its contents rubbish for about a hundred and forty years. *What is in it.* **They are returns.** Every one has a column headed for the person, institution or thing engaged in the matter, filled in at the other end, in ink, in a hand that is not hers, with a day on it, **and the day is the day the column was filled and not the day it was sent, and there is no space on the sheet for the second day.** And: **that is the only document in the world that says how a box got filled, and I have never thrown one away, and I have never shown one to anybody, and I have never told anybody what is in it, and there are about nine sheets deep and every one of them is the only one of its kind. And I have just told you.** He does not ask to see one. *You are not going to show me.* **No. You are the second person in a hundred and forty years to have asked me what a word on a form means and you came back and asked me nothing, and a man who comes back and asks nothing is the only kind of person in this empire it is safe to tell anything to at all.** And she will not take one out, because there is one in that basket about the street he used to live on, and if it goes into a man's hand then in about four years somebody can hold it up beside a sheet of hers and ask her to explain the difference, and that person would be him, about himself and his wife and his boy, and she has got no way of stopping him and would not want to. *And if I wanted one.* **You would have to come and ask me for it in a room with two people in it, and there is no form for that, and I would have to say yes in front of the other one, and you did not ask, and I noticed that you did not ask and I want it entered that I noticed.** Then the back room: *That door has been open the whole time you have been talking.* It has, a couple of inches, halfway along, every working day for nine years, and she has been past it about two thousand times. **A person who opens a room nobody has asked her about is a person who has given herself a duty, and a person who has given herself a duty cannot stop**, and one of those is the reason there is a paper about a winter in a drawer in her own room that she has never told the court about. *You have not looked at it.* No. **He nodded at it and did not ask her what was in it, and that was the second time in about ten minutes that a person had stood in front of her and failed to ask her a question, and it is the whole of the reason she has said as much as she has said.** Then the price, again, in full: **You asked me in the first week of the tenth month and I answered you, and the answer was true, and the answer was that nothing has been done to you, and you have been carrying the price of a true answer since then, and I am not going to be the reason that is a strange thing to be carrying.** And: **So there is no document in this empire that says you asked me anything, and you asked me something, and I answered you, and you are the only person who knows it, and I am the only other one, and in about four years neither of us can prove a word of it, and I would like that written down somewhere and I am not going to write it anywhere.** Then the ordinary trade, nine more copies, a man of about fifty at a minute past, and the finding: **about four hundred a year and not one of them is a question, and the reason is not that the people who come to a counter do not want to know things. It is that a person who wants to know something at a counter does not come to a counter, they come to a counter to buy fourpence worth of paper, and they would be ashamed to stand there and use up a minute of a clerk's time for nothing.** And her own register on the top shelf of the second press, **whose fourth column has had no heading over it for nine years and she has not filled it and nobody has ever asked her why, and that is a column in a book on a flood terrace and it is not a column in a book about four hundred and thirty miles up this river and she is not going to stand at a window and pretend the two of them are one thing.** Then her own book with no office on it, the day on it, read back twice, the second slower, and the reason she has written eleven times in nine years: **A hand of mine is the only hand anybody is going to be able to put side by side in about four years, and I have now done this for the twelfth time and I have not stopped and I am not going to.** And, at the foot, a second line, the only second line she has ever written in it, four words, and not a rule: **He came back and asked me nothing.**

## Chapter 214 — remainder of the entry

He goes up the road because the count of the works is due and the water is due and there is nothing else in a closed season, and it takes about half an hour at the pace he walks. **The shed is about nine foot by eleven**, a bench along the north wall, a lamp not lit until about the seventh hour, and it is the third hour of the afternoon. He lets her do the ordinary things, **because a book-keeper who interrupts a foreman in the first two minutes of a visit does not get to the fourth minute, and the fourth minute is where anything happens.** The count of the works, the water, the hold, **the factor's letter, not going to be answered, and every day it is not answered is a day nobody has been entered**, the nineteen reasons read out and not the names with the second reading slower. **A person is entitled to make a refusal once and have it kept and not be troubled about it again, and there is no form anywhere in this empire that entitles anybody to be troubled twice.** Before the satchel comes in: a man of about fifty-five, one of the four, at the bench with a drum between his knees taking a third link out of a chain with a nail, because the smith is at Lowcross and Lowcross is a bridge and the bridge is shut; and a man of about forty from the third gang asks him a question about the water standing where he is, and he looks at the bench for about two seconds, and **Halla Wray answers it from behind him without turning round and in about four seconds flat, in a foreman's voice, in the plainest words there are, and the man of about forty accepts it and goes back to what he was doing, and nobody in that shed notices anything at all.** The book-keeper saw the whole of it and did not understand it for about two hours afterwards and has not entirely understood it since, and wrote nothing down then or afterwards. Then a man of about thirty from a district works with a satchel, standing in the shed in a way that says he has been in a great many of them that month. *What I have to know is who I enter.* *That depends what you want to enter him for.* *I have a column and a day, and last season the column came back with a household in it and I could not put a household on a list of works.* *I put what the man does. That is the answer and you can write it down.* **And there are four of yours that do not come to the shed and that I cannot ask, and I have been told that in about four minutes by a man in a store and not by a person, and I am asking the person.** **The shed went quiet for about a minute and a half, and Rennick Adley stood against the north wall with the ledger under his arm and did not move, and there are about nine men in that shed and not one of them has ever heard a foreman of fifty-one be asked a question about the four by anybody at all, ever, including the four.** And she answers it, in the ordinary way, standing, in about a minute and a half: **Because if I ask one of them I have asked one of them, and then there are three, and the three will be asked by whoever comes next, because a man who has been asked can be asked again, and that is the only door anybody has ever opened on a person who cannot read a paragraph. And the fourth will be found out by arithmetic.** *That is a bad reason.* **It is the reason and it is mine and I have had it since the third month, and I have not said it to a book-keeper since the second week of the eleventh month of the year after and I have just said it to a man with a satchel who had not been in the room when I decided not to say it, and I would like it noticed that the only way a sentence of mine comes out of my mouth is when somebody asks me a question about the works.** The man writes it down and then asks a second question, and the second question is not about the four: *Before I put my column in — is there anything on that bank I am going to be told about that I have not been told.* **No**, and she tells him the two things she knows of because he has a column and not because she has decided to: a guarantee standing offered and unanswered on about four hundred and forty foot of it with about sixty children under sixteen inside it, no such person has ever come forward, and she has not got the terms of it in her head and is not going to read them out in a shed at the third hour; and a bill at Lowcross at nineteen pounds three and fourpence, unpaid, nobody liable, no line for a bridge in that fund in nineteen years. *That is not my district.* **It is nobody's. That is what a bill with no line against it is.** *That is a bad reason.* And then, from the wall, Rennick Adley hears it. He has wanted that sentence since the third month of this year, and he told a foreman of fifty-one in the seventh month out loud in front of about nine men that the day he asked was the day she stopped saying the half she had got, and she heard him, and he has not asked, and he is not going to. **And it came out in a shed on the fourth day of the fourth week of the twelfth month, out loud, in a room, aimed at a man with a satchel who would not remember it in a week, and it went to a stranger.** He could not write it down: a line in the back of that book is a findable thing, and what would be in it is a foreman's own reason for a decision about four men, and a man with a copy of the right page would put it beside a note of a seat and ask why the district's man was told it in a shed and not told to the book-keeper who had been waiting nine months. He could not ask her. And he could not repeat it, which is the one he had not thought of: **The half of a sentence she has got goes to strangers, and it has always gone to strangers**, because a foreman's answer to a question about the works is said to whoever is asking about the works, and a man from a district works is a stranger, and there is no form anywhere in this empire that requires a foreman to give the same answer twice to the same person in the same room, so in a week or so she will be able to say a different thing and both of them will have been true. **So a man who is owed a sentence and is not going to be given it is a man who has it, and cannot use it, and cannot put it anywhere, and has to carry it.** She finds out afterwards, at the bench, with both hands on the ledger and doing nothing with it. *That was a whole sentence and I did not mean to say it in a room.* *I know you did not.* **I have had it since the third month, and I have given it to a district in about a minute and a half, and I have not given it to you, and you know I have not, and I would like it entered that I know you know.** *There is nothing to enter it in, and that is not a complaint.* At the gate, to his back, which she has not done in about nine years: **that was a whole sentence and a foreman who has given a reason away by accident is in a worse position than one who has not, because now there is a copy of it in a satchel going four hundred and thirty miles up this river with nobody's name on it, and I cannot get it back and I would not want to.** *I am not going to write it down.* **I know you are not going to write it down, and I know exactly why, and I have known since the seventh month, and I would have given it to you in the seventh month if you had asked and you did not ask and that was correct and it is still correct and I am not going to say anything else about it.** He walked the half hour back with the ledger and the water on his left and about four hundred yards of it in view the whole way, and wrote nothing down, told nobody, and slept little. **And on the fourth day of the last week of the twelfth month of the year after the year turned, and nobody in that bay noticed the day it turned on, and there is no form anywhere in this empire that requires a person to notice a year, and it is not a form and it is not a day and it is in nobody's book, and the season was shut and the water was down and about nineteen reasons were in a column with a day against every one of them and about four men in that bay still had never been asked anything, and a foreman of fifty-one had said a whole sentence out loud in a room to a stranger, and a book-keeper of thirty-eight had heard it and could not do one single thing with it.**

## Chapter 215 — remainder of the entry

**She walked about nine miles in one day and in about eleven hours with two stops, and she had the address in her head and not on a paper, because there is a form in this empire for the address of a place a person may be served at and there is not one for the address of a person who is not in a matter.** What she decided in the second week of the eleventh month of the year after, standing in a room about nine miles from a counter: **I cannot get out of the box, and I am going to be the person who knows how it was filled, and I am not going to be a person who knows it alone.** Four people. She has asked one. At the door: **I did not send for anybody. There is no form in this empire for a person who comes on her own account and I have looked and you are the second one I have done this to.** Marek Kest, thirty-eight, a rented room with a table in it, a book with no office on it, about four feet of a wall he does not use, no office, no fund, no register, **and a bar of his own that came off him on the twelfth of the ninth month of last year on an application of one line which he wrote himself.** Tamsin Rook, twenty-four, in the room because two instruments require it and they are not one instrument: **a paper made in a form in four days with a day on it, and a watch that does not name him and does not name her and requires only that a person who is not from the office that cannot be named be present.** He has eleven lines in the book with a day against the sheet and not against the lines, and a twelfth line with nothing on it; she looks at both and asks about neither, which he notices, and which is the reason he sits down. *What have you come for.* **What does the word *engaged* mean.** *Ask it again.* **I have just asked it. I came nine miles to ask it. I have never asked anybody anything in my life that took me nine miles and I am not going to stand on a stair and ask it twice.** *Then ask it in here, because I want it out loud in a room and not on a step.* She asks it in the room, standing, hat in hand, and it is the fourth time in about a hundred and forty years that one person in this empire has asked another person what a word on a form meant, and the first three were a court room in the fifth month of the year after that wanted a record, a clerk's window in the first week of the tenth, and a desk in the second week of the eleventh, **and two of the three were accidents and nobody chose either of them, and this one was on purpose and nobody sent her.** He says the count out loud, because a number a person says can be held to, and answers in about four minutes. **I can tell you what it has been used to do. I cannot tell you what it means**, and the difference between those two things is the whole of the question and there is nobody in this empire who can close it. A roll of households does not list rights, holdings or claims; that is on the face of the third line and is older than he is; the office asks for a person; the only question there is an answer to is who is on the roll; the answer is a household; the box takes a household because the box takes a thing; it says *the person, institution or thing engaged in the matter* and does not say which of the three is wanted, and there is no box anywhere in about nine hundred buildings that will only take one of them. **And nobody has ever needed the word defined, because a box that will take a thing can always be filled in, and a box that will only take a person cannot. Not because anybody is keeping it from you. Because a thing fits in a box and a person does not, and a form is a thing, and a form cannot be asked what any of its words mean, and a form is the only thing in this empire that has never once been asked and the only thing in this empire that has never once said no.** Then: **it means you are on a roll at a door**, and the difference between a roll at a door and her is a thing about four hundred and thirty miles away in a lane, walked once a year with a sheet on a board and a basket, and **she does not know what the fourth column of her own roll is for, and there is no fund and no form for a fund, and nobody has ever been to ask her anything, and that is the whole end of it.** And: *I have been on her list. She is the one who told me.* **Then she is the only person alive who can say how your box got filled, and she cannot be asked, and that is not anybody's plot.** Then Tamsin Rook says the price and then the other half of it: **I am going to say the price out loud and I have said it before and I am not going to be thanked for it, and there is no form anywhere in this empire that requires a person to be thanked. A person who asks a question about a form in a room becomes a person somebody has answered. Not the person the question is about. The one who asked.** And she has said the price of being a party out loud ten times and nobody has thanked her once and she did not expect to be thanked once and would like it written down that she did not. **And nobody has ever said the other half of it, and I have only just worked it out at about the fourth minute of this: Every person who has ever answered anybody about a form in this empire has been a clerk. A clerk is findable. A clerk has a window, or a counter, or a room, or eleven books on a shelf, and in about four years a man with a copy of the right page can walk in and put a line under that clerk's name and be answered. And he is not a clerk. He has no office and no register and nothing in this empire he could be looked up in. He is the first person in this matter who is answerable and cannot be found, and the reason he cannot be found is that nobody was ever going to have to look him up, and that is the same sentence as the reason the office cannot tell you how many of you there are.** *Say that again slowly.* **No. A number a person says can be held to, and if I say it again then there are two of them and they will not be identical, and I am not going to be a person who improves a thing she has already said in a room.** *Then say the part that is about him.* **There is nothing anybody can instruct him about, and nothing anybody can pay him for, and nothing anybody can thank him for, and that is the reason he is the most dangerous person in this matter and it is also the reason nobody in it can protect him**, and she said both halves in the third week of the tenth month of the year after and is not going to soften the second one now. Then his answer, standing: **I am a man with a bar that came off him on a one-line application and I have read every form in this business for about four years, and there is no definition of that word in any of them**, and there is not going to be one, because the moment there is one a keeper of a roll has to decide whether a household is engaged in a matter, and a keeper cannot be asked what a word on a form means, and a keeper has no instrument and no protection and a basket once a year. **The word is not undefined by accident. The word is undefined on purpose, and not by anybody. It is undefined the way a gap in a hedge is a shape rather than a hole.** And: **you are engaged in the matter in the only sense the form can hold, which is that you are the nearest thing to a person in it.** Then she asks whether she may write it down, he says no, she says why not, and she finishes his sentence for him: **Because a hand of mine is now the only hand anybody is going to be able to put side by side in about four years, and you have no hand to put beside mine because you have no office, and I have a book of eleven houses that nobody pays me to keep, and there is nothing anywhere in this empire that would hold this except that book.** *You have been in rooms with about four thousand entries in eleven minute books and you did not know that.* And I have known it since the second week of the ninth month, standing at a counter, where a clerk of thirty-four told me what a representative is and would not write it down, and she was right, and **this morning I have found somebody else who is carrying the same thing for the same reason.** Then she writes it at the table, on a page at the back of the book of eleven houses, in her own hand, with the day on it, **and she reads it back once and does not read it twice**, because the second reading is a rule about something a room decided and this is not a room and there are only two of us in it and one of us cannot be found. And then four lines at the foot of the page, which are the reason, and she turns the book round and pushes it across the table so that he can read them, which nobody has ever asked her to do. *A person who is paid to read is a person who can be told to stop. There is no post whose job is to ask. — N. Kest, second day of the first week of the first month of the year after next.* And the close: the year after next had been four days old, nobody had thanked her and she had not asked, **she was answerable, and he was answerable, and there were four askings in a hundred and forty years and no post and no plan, and it would not happen again by itself.**

## Chapter 216 — remainder of the entry

He could not sleep much on the night of the second day of the first week and that was nothing unusual, and on the fourth day of the second week he wrote a thing down and put a day against it in about nine seconds and had been about four days deciding to, and the whole of the four days was the question of whether doing it was fair to the woman. He asks her to answer in a room, in words, with a date, **and it is the rule of the twelfth month of last year and it is not a form and it is not on her office and it is the only instrument either of us owns, and I am going to put it in writing with a day on it and I am not going to write the day against the line.** Then the choice, in ink: *Either I write down what I said to a woman in this room on the second day of the first week of this month, and stand in front of whoever comes and be asked what I meant by it, or I do not. If I write it down then it is a document and I am a person with a document, and in about four years a man with a copy of the right page can put it beside the return that made her and ask me why a man with no office answered a question about a box. — M.K.* She reads it twice and puts her pen down beside it, which she did last time. *My answer is no, and the date is today, and I am going to give you the reason and then I am not going to discuss it.* **A document with your hand at the foot of it does not stay about you. That is the whole of what I have been trying to tell you since the second month and you have not believed a word of it.** In about four years there are two of them in the same thing: a sheet of a man's hand saying what he said to a woman about a word on a box, and a return with a column filled in at the other end with a woman's name in it that a clerk filled in in about nine seconds in the fifth month of the year after. A man with a copy of the right page does not have to know anything about either of them. **If you write that down she is findable from a page of a man with no office, and you would be doing to her the identical thing that was done to her in the fifth month, in about nine seconds, by a person who was not a villain and who is sitting at this table.** He does not argue, and she watches him not argue, **and about four hours after that she would work out for the second time in three days that it is the only evidence she has ever had of anybody's judgment and she had still not decided what to do with it.** *That is a good reason.* **It is a fact. And you have asked me this once before and I refused you in the second week of the ninth month of the year after, and you wrote that one in order to be refused as well, and you told me so afterwards, and it is the only reason I have believed a document you wrote.** *So I have written two things down in a book with no office on it in order to be refused, and I have had two real answers, and there is no office to appeal to.* **There is no post in this empire whose business it is to ask whether I was right. That is not a complaint about you.** Then the wrist, and: **Then it goes in a room and not on a page, and I am not going to thank you for telling me that you have nothing, because there is no form anywhere in this empire that requires a person to be thanked and I have asked you in advance not to and you are not going to.** Then the third thing, the one he had come to say: **I am not going to go and tell her.** A woman of twenty walked about nine miles and asked him a question and he could not answer it and has been answerable for four days for a thing he does not know, and the answer is nothing, **because there is no fund and there is no form for a fund and I have got about a fortnight's money in a drawer and a book with no office on it.** He cannot pay her, cannot buy her a copy at fourpence, cannot pay her a shilling for a question, cannot send her four hundred and thirty miles with a carriage, and **there is nothing anybody can instruct me about and nothing anybody can pay me for, and I said that out loud in this room in the third week of the tenth month of the year after and I am not going to improve it by finding a way to be generous with a person who cannot be protected.** And: **in the second week of the eleventh month she decided to be the person who knows how the box got filled and not to be a person who knows it alone, and the minute I walk about nine miles and tell her what she has become, she has a person to hand it to, and that is the end of the thing she decided and it would be me ending it for her.** *She has three other people.* **She has two other people and one of them is four hundred and thirty miles up this river with a board and a basket and no fund, and the other is four hundred and thirty miles down it at a window, and I have not met either of them and I am not going to, and about four years from now she is going to find out that she chose well or badly and I am not going to be what finds out.** Then she names the other thing, at about the seventh hour: *You could stand in the room where the boxes get filled. Nine miles.* **No.** *That was fast.* **I have been thinking about it for four days and I have got a reason and it is yours. Being served is a party. A party is the only person who can be told what a thing is going to be used for, and I do not know what that thing is going to be used for, and a man with no office and no register who walks into a building off the old river road and asks a clerk what a box is for is asking to be served, and about four years later a man with a copy of the right page will be able to say that he came asking, and there is no way in this empire to come out of a party once you are in it. And I have said the twelfth line is going to stay empty because there is no post whose job is to ask, and I am not going to be the man who walks into a building to find out whether there is one.** *That is consistent. It is also the first decision you have made in about two years that you did not make in order to be caught at it, and I have not got a rule about what to do with that, and I am not going to invent one at about the seventh hour on a day when I have been asked a question I cannot answer.* Then: she is gone six minutes, because of the paper and not the watch, and he says which one it was out loud to an empty room, **because a form gets merged by being inconvenient and he had been letting two instruments get merged in his own head for about four days.** A paper made in a form in four days with a day on it; the watch does not send anybody anywhere. Then he opens the book. Eleven lines with a day against the sheet, a twelfth with nothing on it, and he has not read the eleventh line out loud to anybody since the third week of the ninth month of the year after, and the eleventh line is the one about what a person of twenty has been put into, and she read it in that room and let him, and neither of them said anything about it for about a minute. **The eleventh line had stopped being a question. It had become a fact, and a fact on a line in a book with no office on it is the worst of the two, because a question can be answered and a fact can only be repeated.** He reads it to himself once, out loud, in about four seconds, and shuts the book with the twelfth line still empty. And when she comes back: *I did not read you the eleventh line while you were out.* **I know you did not. I heard you not read it through the door, and I am not going to ask you why and I would like it entered that I did not ask.** *Because you would be asking me to say a thing about a woman of twenty out loud in a room, and the only person in the room who could contradict me is her, and she is not in the room, and I would like it entered that I noticed that too and that I still did not read it.* Then the book with the choice in it goes into the book with no office on it at the back, after the four definitions, with the day on it: **the rule of the twelfth month used once, in writing, with a day on it, the answer no, given in about a minute by a person who had already thought about it, he did not argue, and it is the second time.** *And I have not said a word about the twelve questions on your sheet, and the twelfth line is still empty, and I would like it read back to me now, in this room, in words, whether you think that is cowardice.* **It is not, and it is the second time I have said that to you and I am not going to be thanked for saying it, and the twelfth line is still empty, and I have not ruled a third box and I am not going to.** And the last: *Four.* **Four. Four in about a hundred and forty years and no post and no plan, and three of them are a year old or more, and the fourth is a fortnight old, and it was the first one anybody did on purpose, and I am not going to be one of the three of us who is standing in a room saying it will not happen again by itself while the one person it happened to has her wage held about four hundred and thirty miles from here.**

## Chapter 217 — remainder of the entry

She had known since the fourth day of the fourth week of the twelfth month that she had said a whole sentence out loud in a room to a man with a satchel, and had about five weeks to do something about it and had not, and worked out on the morning of the second day why: **there was nothing in the world she could have done about it.** Then a man of about forty from the third gang asks a man of about fifty-five a question about the water, standing where he is, and the man of about fifty-five looks at the bench for about two seconds, and Halla Wray answers it from behind him without turning round and in about four seconds flat, in a foreman's voice, in the plainest words there are, and the man of about forty accepts it and goes back to what he was doing, and nobody in that shed notices anything at all, and that is what a foreman is. **She has been doing that for nine years. She did it that week in the fourth week of the twelfth month, and she has done it since she was forty-two, and nobody has ever asked her to, and about nine men have seen it, and nobody has ever written it down, and she is the only person in this empire who knows it**, and she worked that out at about the ninth hour standing in a bay with a list of works in front of her. *There.* **There is the answer to the question.
